## Q37. For γ∈(0,2), is a γ-Liouville-quantum-gravity geodesic metrically removable: can…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.56) · **Collection** 1

For γ∈(0,2), is a γ-Liouville-quantum-gravity geodesic metrically removable: can the ambient metric be recovered from path lengths off the geodesic by metric gluing?

**Context.** Origin: The intersection and geodesic-star questions are joint with Konstantinos Kavvadias. The removability and dynamical-percolation questions arise from Bhatia’s own papers.

**Source.** Manan Bhatia. *Two flavours of random geometry: geodesics in the directed landscape and Liouville quantum gravity*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Scott Sheffield. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/97b14590-0ca1-4281-adbc-d5eb924836dd/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/97b14590-0ca1-4281-adbc-d5eb924836dd/content#page=1) Location: §5.7, Question 5.56, p.198. Follow-on evidence: Still explicitly posed in the thesis. The 2026 publication of the associated paper retains Question 54; conformal removability is a different, already settled property. No metric-removability resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2404.02862v1#S7) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1214/25-AAP2256) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1812.03913)

## Q39. Give each vertex of ℤ^(2) an independent Exp(1) weight, refreshed independently …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 8.2) · **Collection** 1

Give each vertex of ℤ^(2) an independent Exp(1) weight, refreshed independently at rate one. Do exceptional times exist at which an up-right bi-infinite maximizing path, other than a horizontal or vertical line, occurs? If so, determine the Hausdorff dimension of those times.

**Context.** Origin: The intersection and geodesic-star questions are joint with Konstantinos Kavvadias. The removability and dynamical-percolation questions arise from Bhatia’s own papers.

**Source.** Manan Bhatia. *Two flavours of random geometry: geodesics in the directed landscape and Liouville quantum gravity*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Scott Sheffield. Follow-on evidence: The exponential model remains posed in the latest associated revision. The September 28, 2026 zero-dimensionality result concerns Brownian LPP, not this model; no exponential-model resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.12293) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35103)

## Q45. Let G_(L) be G(n,p) conditioned on N_(H)≤η𝔼[N_(H)], where N_(H) counts H-copies.…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 1

Let G_(L) be G(n,p) conditioned on N_(H)≤η𝔼[N_(H)], where N_(H) counts H-copies. Take η\_(H)<η<1 in the replica-symmetric range, p≫n^(−1/m_(2)(H)), and q=η^(1/e(H))p. For every ε>0, can G_(L) be coupled between G(n,(1−ε)q) and G(n,(1+ε)q) with high probability? Here m_(2)(H)=max_(F⊆H, v(F)≥3)(e(F)−1)/(v(F)−2).

**Context.** Origin: Chin poses these finer questions after his lower-tail structure theorem, also developed in his 2025 paper.

**Source.** Byron Chin. *Finding thresholds and structure in discrete random models*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Elchanan Mossel. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/7d81108c-f89c-43b3-a8f7-075f0b6ea89e/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/7d81108c-f89c-43b3-a8f7-075f0b6ea89e/content#page=1) Location: §5.1.1, Question 5.1, p.107; η\_(H) defined pp.106–107. Search-qualified status: The dissertation explicitly leaves these open. Targeted searches found the 2025 structural theorem, not a solution to either finer question.

**Further links.** [1](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1002/rsa.70028)

## Q191. For d ≥ 2, let P_(N)⁰ be the canonical law of d-dimensional Brownian motion on […

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1.3) · **Collection** 2

For d ≥ 2, let P_(N)⁰ be the canonical law of d-dimensional Brownian motion on [0, 1], starting at zero and conditioned to have all nonconstant Stratonovich iterated integrals through order N equal to zero. Does P_(N)⁰ ⇒ δ₀ in the uniform topology on C₀([0, 1], ℝ^(d)) as N → ∞?

**Context.** Origin: Declaration identifies Chapter 4 as original research arising from dialogue with Terry Lyons. The chapter expressly proposes these conjectures. Degree/publication year is 2018 per the author’s thesis listing; cover is dated August 2017. Status evidence for this section, checked 5 October 2026: Habermann’s 2021 JLMS paper explicitly reiterates the Brownian-loop conjecture while proving the distinct Gaussian iterated-Kolmogorov-loop analogue. The author’s publications through 2026 and targeted signature/loop/conjecture searches yielded no resolution. The thesis proves the comparison only at N=1. Status source 1: https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/jlms.12384
Status source 2: https://warwick.ac.uk/fac/sci/statistics/staff/academic-research/habermann/publications/

**Source.** Karen Habermann. *Geometry of sub-Riemannian diffusion processes*. University of Cambridge, 2018. Advisor(s): James Norris. [primary source](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/23395d65-5112-4485-95e9-0fb3ad75f796/content) · [record](https://www.maths.cam.ac.uk/postgrad/cca/people/alumni.html) Location: Conjecture 4.1.3, printed p. 81; canonical disintegration, p. 82. Status: see the section-wide dated evidence above.

**Further links.** [1](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/jlms.12384) · [2](https://warwick.ac.uk/fac/sci/statistics/staff/academic-research/habermann/publications/)

*Duplicate of Q0095331cf464021a4a06.*


<a id="q192"></a>

## Q192. For independent standard Brownian motions B¹, B² starting at zero, condition on …

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.2.3) · **Collection** 2

For independent standard Brownian motions B¹, B² starting at zero, condition on ∫₀¹(B_(t)¹)^(N − 1) ∘ dB_(t)² = 0 using the canonical density disintegration. Does the conditional law of B¹ converge weakly in C₀([0, 1], ℝ) to the zero path as N → ∞?

**Context.** Origin: Declaration identifies Chapter 4 as original research arising from dialogue with Terry Lyons. The chapter expressly proposes these conjectures. Degree/publication year is 2018 per the author’s thesis listing; cover is dated August 2017. Status evidence for this section, checked 5 October 2026: Habermann’s 2021 JLMS paper explicitly reiterates the Brownian-loop conjecture while proving the distinct Gaussian iterated-Kolmogorov-loop analogue. The author’s publications through 2026 and targeted signature/loop/conjecture searches yielded no resolution. The thesis proves the comparison only at N=1. Status source 1: https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/jlms.12384
Status source 2: https://warwick.ac.uk/fac/sci/statistics/staff/academic-research/habermann/publications/

**Source.** Karen Habermann. *Geometry of sub-Riemannian diffusion processes*. University of Cambridge, 2018. Advisor(s): James Norris. [primary source](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/23395d65-5112-4485-95e9-0fb3ad75f796/content) · [record](https://www.maths.cam.ac.uk/postgrad/cca/people/alumni.html) Location: Conjecture 4.2.3, printed p. 84; definition of Q_N and Lemma 4.2.2, pp. 82–84. Status: see the section-wide dated evidence above.

**Further links.** [1](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/jlms.12384) · [2](https://warwick.ac.uk/fac/sci/statistics/staff/academic-research/habermann/publications/)

*Duplicate of Q11a52318057a76bc65a4.*

**Result (2026-10-09).** Under the canonical density disintegration, the Brownian path conditioned on one power integral being zero converges to the zero path in the uniform topology. Every fixed-radius escape probability decays faster than any prescribed exponential in the power. Exact single-integral question only; the full-signature and comparison questions Q191 and Q193 remain unresolved.

[Proof](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/probability/Q192-brownian-power-conditioning.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/discrete/review-Q192.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/probability/probability-certificates.json). This is a research proposal with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed result.

## Q196. Fix 0 < Q < 2 and a whole-plane GFF h normalized by h₁(0) = 0. For a dyadic squa…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.6.5) · **Collection** 2

Fix 0 < Q < 2 and a whole-plane GFF h normalized by h₁(0) = 0. For a dyadic square S of side |S| and center v_(S), set M_(h)(S) = e^(h_(|S|/2)(v_(S)))|S|^(Q), where h_(r) is the circle average. Let 𝒮\_(h)¹ contain squares with M_(h)(S) ≤ 1 and M_(h)(S^(′)) > 1 for every strict dyadic ancestor; join squares sharing a nondegenerate boundary segment. Is simple random walk on this graph almost surely transient?

**Context.** Origin: The square model is joint with Gwynne, Holden, and Remy; DLA with Gwynne. The questions predate and enter the thesis.

**Source.** Joshua William Pfeffer. *Frontiers of Liouville Quantum Gravity*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2020. Advisor(s): Scott Sheffield. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/3616d613-b621-4195-81af-120786d5927c/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/3616d613-b621-4195-81af-120786d5927c/content#page=1) Location: Conjecture 5.6.5, printed p. 194; model, pp. 143–144. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Conjecture 6.6 remains in the associated primary paper. Checked Gwynne–Sung 2025 on reflection at infinity: its transience announcement concerns a different recursive planar-map model, not this square graph. Author publication list and targeted searches through October 2026 found no square-graph resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.09111) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2506.18827) · [3](https://math.uchicago.edu/~ewain/)

*Duplicate of Qa5610ffcae627ea05f68.*

## Q197. For the DLA tree X_m after m edges, does lim_(m→∞) log(diam_(X_m)(X_m))/log m ex…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 8.8.1) · **Collection** 2

For the DLA tree X_m after m edges, does lim_(m→∞) log(diam_(X_m)(X_m))/log m exist almost surely, and what is its value? Diameter uses internal tree distance.

**Context.** Origin: The square model is joint with Gwynne, Holden, and Remy; DLA with Gwynne. The questions predate and enter the thesis.

**Source.** Joshua William Pfeffer. *Frontiers of Liouville Quantum Gravity*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2020. Advisor(s): Scott Sheffield. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The February 2021 primary-paper revision and Annals of Probability publication retain these as Problems 8.1–8.2. Searches for later work and Gwynne’s current publication list found no resolution; the proved ambient-distance growth exponent does not determine internal tree diameter or ends.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B6: unresolved): No new intrinsic-diameter exponent theorem obtained. Remaining/limits: Existence and value of the almost-sure internal diameter exponent remain. [Report](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/1901.06860) · [2](https://math.uchicago.edu/~ewain/)

## Q266. Does every poset P without an infinite antichain contain a chain C satisfying |C…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1.10) · **Collection** 3

Does every poset P without an infinite antichain contain a chain C satisfying |C∖D| ≥ |D∖C| for every chain D in P, with the sizes compared as cardinals?

**Context.** Metadata note: Repository and title page give October 2025; the deposited declaration is dated 5 February 2026. Origin: Poset and tournament questions are Hollom’s. Sharp tails are joint with Portier; parity-sensitive vector balancing with Portier and Souza. Relevant papers predate inclusion in the thesis.

## Q267. For which uncountable cardinals κ does every vacillating poset P of size κ, with…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.6.2) · **Collection** 3

For which uncountable cardinals κ does every vacillating poset P of size κ, with no infinite antichain, admit a chain meeting every class of some antichain partition of P? Vacillating means no saturated chain, or its reverse, is a lexicographic sum of countably many infinite chains having no infinite increasing sequence. A chain is saturated if no outside element can be inserted between two of its elements while preserving a chain.

**Context.** Metadata note: Repository and title page give October 2025; the deposited declaration is dated 5 February 2026. Origin: Poset and tournament questions are Hollom’s. <a id="q270"></a>

## Q270. Take two copies of \$T_n\$, the tournament on \$\\{1,\ldots,n\\}\$ with arc \$i\to j\$ e…

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.6.4) · **Collection** 3

Take two copies of \$T_n\$, the tournament on \$\\{1,\ldots,n\\}\$ with arc \$i\to j\$ exactly when \$i&lt;j\$, and join corresponding vertices by one undirected vertical edge. Retain every horizontal arc and vertical edge independently with probability \$1/2\$. Is \$\mathbb P(u^{(0)}\to v^{(0)})\ge\mathbb P(u^{(0)}\to v^{(1)})\$ for every \$n,u,v\$, allowing vertical traversal in either direction?

**Context.** Metadata note: Repository and title page give October 2025; the deposited declaration is dated 5 February 2026. Origin: Poset and tournament questions are Hollom’s. Sharp tails are joint with Portier; parity-sensitive vector balancing with Portier and Souza. Relevant papers predate inclusion in the thesis.

**Source.** Lawrence Albert Hollom. *Extremal, Probabilistic, and Infinitary Problems in Combinatorics*. University of Cambridge, 2025. Advisor(s): Béla Bollobás. [primary source](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/a0069f85-e810-476b-ba41-c2babc9c2f0b/content) · [record](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/items/d9fa2d8e-7393-49d3-933e-0dc3229c2efa) Location: Conjecture 6.6.4, printed p. 158; model D₇ in Section 6.4, pp. 152–153.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ejc.2025.104188) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.01790) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2506.22284)

**Result (2026-10-09).** The independent-edge, bidirectional-vertical bunkbed inequality holds for every transitive tournament. The proof allows any common horizontal retention probability and independent vertex-dependent vertical probabilities; at probability one half the gap for u<v is at least 2^(-(v-u+2)). Transitive tournaments and the stated independent-edge model. Arbitrary acyclic graphs and conditioned paired-edge models are outside the result.

[Proof](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/probability/Q270-transitive-tournament-bunkbed.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/discrete/review-Q270.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/round-2026-10-09/probability/Q270-certificates.json). This is a research proposal with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed result.

## Q275. On \$\mathbb Z^d\$, \$d\ge2\$, is there \$0<\alpha_c(d)<\infty\$ separating almost-sur…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.1.2) · **Collection** 3

On \$\mathbb Z^d\$, \$d\ge2\$, is there \$0<\alpha_c(d)<\infty\$ separating almost-surely finite final clusters for \$\alpha>\alpha_c\$ from occurrence of infinite final clusters for \$0<\alpha<\alpha_c\$?

**Context.** Origin: Original constant-freezing questions, also in the author’s 2013 preprint, published in 2014. Shared setup for questions 275, 276, 277, 278: In percolation with constant freezing, initially closed edges open at rate \$1\$ while both endpoints are warm. Each connected cluster freezes at rate \$\alpha\$, independently of its size, permanently blocking unopened incident edges. Write \$\mu_{G,\alpha}\$ for the final open-edge law. Increasing events remain true when open edges are added.

**Source.** Edward John Mottram. *The geometry of non-Markovian interacting systems*. University of Cambridge, 2015. Advisor(s): Nathanaël Berestycki. [primary source](https://www.repository.cam.ac.uk/bitstreams/72e98faf-8e12-4374-9a2e-c0c8ad6840e1/download) · [record](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/items/6b7c7424-511e-4174-bbe9-1ed148d79015) Location: Conjecture 3.1.2, printed p. 37 (PDF p. 47).

## Q280. Is \$q_{p,d}=\tilde q_{p,d}\$ for every \$p\ge1,d\ge3\$?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.1.2) · **Collection** 3

Is \$q_{p,d}=\tilde q_{p,d}\$ for every \$p\ge1,d\ge3\$?

**Context.** Origin: Original equal-weight quantization questions, also in the author’s 2024 paper. Shared setup for questions 280, 281, 282: Use Euclidean \$p\$-Wasserstein distance \$W_p\$ on \$\mathbb R^d\$. For a probability measure \$\mu\$, let \$e_{p,n}(\mu)=\inf_{\\#\operatorname{supp}\nu\le n}W_p(\mu,\nu)\$ over probability measures, and \$\tilde e_{p,n}(\mu)=\inf_{x_1,\ldots,x_n}W_p(\mu,n^{-1}\sum_i\delta_{x_i})\$. For uniform \$U\$ on \$[0,1]^d\$, put \$q_{p,d}=\inf_n n^{1/d}e_{p,n}(U)\$ and \$\tilde q_{p,d}=\inf_n n^{1/d}\tilde e_{p,n}(U)\$.

**Source.** Filippo Quattrocchi. *Optimal transport methods for kinetic equations, boundary value problems, and discretization of measures*. Institute of Science and Technology Austria, 2025. Advisor(s): Jan Maas. [primary source](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/download/20563/20653/2025_quattrocchi_filippo_thesis.pdf) · [record](https://research-explorer.ista.ac.at/record/20563) Location: Conjecture 5.1.2 and equation (5.1.6), printed pp. 185–186 (PDF pp. 197–198).

## Q287. Are the following conditions equivalent: (i) \$\gamma\ge1\$ and \$a_k>1\$ for all su…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.4.1) · **Collection** 3

Are the following conditions equivalent: (i) \$\gamma\ge1\$ and \$a_k>1\$ for all sufficiently large \$k\$; (ii) \$\gamma\ge1\$ and \$\gamma_{\rm PS}>1\$; (iii) \$(X,\Delta)\$ admits a Kähler–Einstein metric satisfying \$\operatorname{Ric}(\omega)=\omega+[\Delta]\$? Conditions (i) and (ii) are Gibbs polystability and uniform Gibbs polystability; (iii) is equivalent to K-polystability.

**Context.** Metadata note: Doctor of Technology, defended 21 August 2026; the department is jointly operated with the University of Gothenburg. Origin: Joint Andreasson–Berman–Svensson conjectures, first formulated in November 2025; Berman proposed symmetry breaking, developed collaboratively. Shared setup for questions 287, 288: Let \$(X,\Delta)\$ be a smooth projective log Fano pair of dimension at least two: \$\Delta\$ is an effective rational divisor and \$L=-(K_X+\Delta)\$ is ample. Assume \$G=\operatorname{Aut}\_0(X,\Delta)\$ is reductive and nontrivial. For integers \$k\$ clearing denominators, set \$N_k=h^0(X,kL)\$, choose a basis \$(s_i)\$, and put \$D_k=k^{-1}\operatorname{div}(\det(s_i(x_j)))\$ on \$X^{N_k}\$ and \$\Delta_{N_k}=\sum_j\pi_j^\*\Delta\$. Write \$a_k=\operatorname{lct}((X^{N_k})\_{\rm ss},\Delta_{N_k};D_k)\$ and \$b_k=\operatorname{lct}(X^{N_k},\Delta_{N_k};D_k)\$, using the diagonal \$G\$-semistable locus for \$L^{\boxtimes N_k}\$. Here \$\operatorname{lct}(Y,B;D)\$ is the supremum of \$c\$ for which \$|s_D|^{-2c}|s_B|^{-2}\$ is locally integrable; smooth choices of norms do not change it. Set \$\gamma_{\rm PS}=\liminf_k a_k\$ and \$\gamma=\liminf_k b_k\$.

**Source.** Ludvig Svensson. *Regularization of divergent integrals in complex geometry*. Chalmers University of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Håkan Samuelsson Kalm (primary); Robert J. Berman (co-supervisor). [primary source](https://research.chalmers.se/publication/552704/file/552704_Fulltext.pdf) · [record](https://www.gu.se/nyheter/ny-matematik-for-divergenta-integraler) Location: Conjecture 4.4.1, printed p. 96; definitions in Section 3.5.5, pp. 85–87, and equation (4.4.2).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16173v4) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.16173) · [3](https://math.au.dk/aktuelt/aktiviteter/event/aktivitet/6943?cHash=2d7e5d9e36ee6b25babb51a2014ad3b0) · [4](https://mingchenxia.github.io/seminar/)

## Q339. Independently sample 2n uniform points in the unit square [0,1]^2 and join them …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Independently sample 2n uniform points in the unit square [0,1]^2 and join them in n predetermined pairs. Let N_n be the maximum number of these segments that are pairwise disjoint, with any intersection counted as crossing. The dissertation states N_n/√n → c almost surely for a deterministic c in [1/2,15]. Determine the exact value of c.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Ashwin Pananjady Martin → Thomas A. Courtade: https://escholarship.org/uc/item/4ft8z7rv Origin: Courtade explicitly leaves the constant as an open problem arising from his own random-segment model, motivated by disjoint routing paths. His earlier sole-authored 2011 note “On Random Line Segments in the Unit Square” proves order bounds; a separate note dated 2011-11-01 already asks for c and guesses c=2. Thus this is doctoral-period research incorporated into the dissertation, not first posed there. The dissertation itself does not endorse a specific value.

**Source.** Thomas Alexander Courtade. *Two Problems in Multiterminal Information Theory*. University of California, Los Angeles, 2012. Advisor(s): Richard D. Wesel. [primary source](https://escholarship.org/content/qt14f3d8vx/qt14f3d8vx.pdf) · [record](https://escholarship.org/uc/item/14f3d8vx) Location: §4.4, Lemma 18 and §4.4.1 Remarks, printed pp. 159–160.

## Q367. For \$d\ge3\$, \$m=1\$, and \$E=\mathbb Z\$, must every gradient phase for every unper…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

For \$d\ge3\$, \$m=1\$, and \$E=\mathbb Z\$, must every gradient phase for every unperturbed simply attractive potential be the gradient law of an ordinary height-function Gibbs measure?

**Context.** Metadata note: PhD year 2003. The regenerated PDF’s May 2019 title date is a compilation artifact; the original arXiv submission and AMS degree notice verify the year. Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Manan Bhatia → Scott Sheffield: https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/97b14590-0ca1-4281-adbc-d5eb924836dd/content#page=1
Scott Sheffield → Amir Dembo: https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0304049v1#page=3 Origin: Sheffield’s own three separate conjectures: integer-height localization, the Gibbs-to-free-energy-minimizer direction, and finite free energy. Later work explicitly attributes them to his dissertation. Shared setup for questions 367, 368, 369: Heights on \$\mathbb Z^d\$ take values in \$E=\mathbb R^m\$ or \$\mathbb Z^m\$. The \$\mathcal L\$-periodic simply attractive potential (SAP) sums nearest-neighbor coordinate interactions \$V_{xy,j}(\phi_j(y)-\phi_j(x))\$, with convex \$V\ge0\$ diverging at both infinities. Permitted perturbations \$\Psi\$ have finite range and \$|H^\Psi_\Lambda|\le cH^\Phi_\Lambda\$, \$c<1\$. Gradient Gibbs laws obey resampling of height differences. A gradient phase is an \$\mathcal L\$-ergodic such law with finite specific free energy (SFE). Write \$S(\mu)\$ for mean slope, \$\sigma(u)=\inf_{S(\nu)=u}\operatorname{SFE}(\nu)\$ over \$\mathcal L\$-invariant gradient laws, and \$U_\Phi=\operatorname{int}\\{\sigma<\infty\\}\$. Here \$\mathcal L\$ is full-rank.

**Source.** Scott Sheffield. *Random surfaces: large deviations principles and gradient Gibbs measure classifications*. Stanford University, 2003. Advisor(s): Amir Dembo. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0304049v1) · [record](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0304049v1#page=3) Location: Section 10.2.2, printed p. 187; definitions in §1.1.3–§1.1.4, pp. 5–8.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0304049v1) · [2](https://www.ams.org/journals/notices/201201/201201-full-issue.pdf) · [3](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/97b14590-0ca1-4281-adbc-d5eb924836dd/content#page=1) · [4](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.01499) · [5](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01499)

## Q371. Fix \$d\ge3\$, a finite spin set \$\mathcal X\$ with at least three elements, symmet…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Fix \$d\ge3\$, a finite spin set \$\mathcal X\$ with at least three elements, symmetric weights \$\psi:\mathcal X^2\to[0,\infty)\$, and vertex weights \$w:\mathcal X\to(0,\infty)\$. Assume a permissive spin \$a\$ exists with \$\psi(a,b)>0\$ for every \$b\$. Set \$Z_G=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal X^{V(G)}}\prod_{ij\in E(G)}\psi(\sigma_i,\sigma_j)\prod_i w(\sigma_i)\$. Do uniformly random bipartite \$d\$-regular graphs attain the largest asymptotic free-energy density \$f=\lim_{n\to\infty}|V(G_n)|^{-1}\mathbb E\log Z_{G_n}\$ among \$d\$-regular graph sequences converging locally to the infinite \$d\$-regular tree, wherever this limit exists? Local convergence means each fixed-radius neighborhood of a uniformly chosen vertex tends in law to the corresponding tree neighborhood.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Brice Huang → Nike Sun: https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/6aabbc9a-661d-4f61-8389-c91f797c7d20/content#page=1
Nike Sun → Amir Dembo: https://statistics.stanford.edu/people/alumni?page=2 Origin: The same new question appears in §1.3.1 of the 2012 joint Dembo–Montanari–Sly–Sun paper underpinning this chapter. It is their generalization beyond the established two-spin case, not an attribution to an older conjecture.

**Source.** Nike Sun. *Gibbs measures and phase transitions on locally tree-like graphs*. Stanford University, 2014. Advisor(s): Amir Dembo. [primary source](https://stacks.stanford.edu/file/ws071cv3491/main-print-augmented.pdf) · [record](https://statistics.stanford.edu/people/alumni?page=2) Location: Unnumbered open question after Proposition 3.1.8, printed p. 66; model and free-energy definitions in §1.1 and Chapter 3.

**Further links.** [1](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/6aabbc9a-661d-4f61-8389-c91f797c7d20/content#page=1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/1207.5500) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1207.5500) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/1203.2602) · [5](https://par.nsf.gov/servlets/purl/10168970)

## Q379. Fix integers \$2\le d_1<\cdots&lt;d_k\$ with \$d_k\ge3\$ and constants \$0<\delta_j<1\$. …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 4

Fix integers \$2\le d_1<\cdots&lt;d_k\$ with \$d_k\ge3\$ and constants \$0<\delta_j<1\$. On \$[n]\$, include each \$d_j\$-set independently with probability \$n^{1-d_j+\delta_j+o(1)}\$, independently across sizes. Observe only the graph joining pairs that share a hyperedge. Put \$\delta_\* =\max_j\delta_j\$. Is recovery of the largest hyperedges \$\mathcal H_k\$ impossible, in the sense that no estimator satisfies \$\mathbb E|\widehat{\mathcal H}\_k\triangle\mathcal H_k|=o(\mathbb E|\mathcal H_k|)\$, whenever \$\delta_\* > (d_k-2)/d_k+2\delta_k/[d_k(d_k-1)]\$? The reverse strict inequality is the proved sufficient condition; the boundary is not specified.

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Austin Daniel Collins → Yury Polyanskiy: https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/groups.shtml
Chenghao Guo → Yury Polyanskiy: https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/data/theses/2026_PHD_Guo.pdf Origin: The thesis’s heterogeneous-converse problem comes from Guo’s own joint work with Alexander Morgan. Their March 2026 paper explicitly proposes optimality for the largest arity; the catalog records that precise special target rather than inventing a universal threshold for intermediate arities.

**Source.** Chenghao Guo. *Signal Recovery in Random Graphical Models with Local Dependencies*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Guy Bresler; Yury Polyanskiy. [primary source](https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/data/theses/2026_PHD_Guo.pdf) · [record](https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/data/theses/2026_PHD_Guo.pdf) Location: §7.5, Open Problem 3, printed p. 74; heterogeneous model and Theorem 1.6, with matching-converse target. The sharp largest-arity conjecture is explicit in the cited joint paper, §8, item 1.

**Further links.** [1](https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/groups.shtml) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.01268) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2603.01268v1)

## Q380. On \$\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^2\$, direct edges from \$v\$ to \$v+e_i\$. Boundary vertices cop…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

On \$\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^2\$, direct edges from \$v\$ to \$v+e_i\$. Boundary vertices copy their single received bit; every interior vertex uses the same Boolean function \$f:\\{0,1\\}^2\to\\{0,1\\}\$. Must the optimal error from \$X_k\$ tend to \$1/2\$, for every \$f\$ and every \$\delta\in(0,1/2)\$?

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Austin Daniel Collins → Yury Polyanskiy: https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/groups.shtml
Anuran Makur → Yury Polyanskiy: https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/data/theses/2019_PHD_Makur.pdf Origin: Joint doctoral questions with Elchanan Mossel and Yury Polyanskiy, developed before filing and retained in later journal publications. Shared setup for questions 380, 381, 382: A uniform root bit propagates along directed edges, each independently flipping its bit with probability \$\delta\in(0,1/2)\$. Vertices apply the specified gates. \$X_k\$ is the entire distance-\$k\$ layer; reconstruction means asymptotic optimal decoding error below \$1/2\$.

**Source.** Anuran Makur. *Information Contraction and Decomposition*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2019. Advisor(s): Lizhong Zheng; Yury Polyanskiy. [primary source](https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/data/theses/2019_PHD_Makur.pdf) · [record](https://people.lids.mit.edu/yp/homepage/data/theses/2019_PHD_Makur.pdf) Location: §5.1, printed p. 200; grid model §5.3.2, pp. 204–205; §5.10, item 6, pp. 264–265.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Let \$L_0=1\$, \$L_k=O(\log k)\$, and each layer-\$k\$ vertex choose \$d\ge3\$ parents independently and uniformly, with replacement, from layer \$k-1\$. ## Q423. Does iterated structural self-adhesivity add four-variable constraints?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 5

Can st^◇(N)≠st^◇◇(N) when |N|=4?

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit questions in the joint 2025 paper. Shared setup for questions 423, 424, 425, 426: For finite N and distinct i,j,k outside K, sg consists of CI patterns satisfying (ij|K)∧(ik|jK)⇔(ik|K)∧(ij|kK). st consists of exact zero patterns of Δ\_h(ij|K)=h(iK)+h(jK)−h(K)−h(ijK), where h(∅)=0 and h is monotone and submodular. f^◇ contains patterns admitting, for every overlap L, an identical-copy extension inside f on N∪N′, N∩N′=L, with independent disjoint remainders given L. Here A⊥B|L abbreviates all ij|K with i∈A,j∈B,L⊆K⊆(A∪B∪L)∖{i,j}; copies preserve both restrictions.

**Source.** Tobias Boege; Janneke H. Bolt; Milan Studený. *Self-adhesivity in lattices of abstract conditional independence models*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.14053) Location: Open problem 2.

**Literature check.** Status, checked 5 October 2026: Explicitly open in 2025; later bounded searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.dam.2024.10.006) · [2](https://cinet.link/data/selfadhe-lattices/) · [3](https://taboege.de/research/)

<a id="q430"></a>

## Q430. Decomposable signed circuits for determinantal laws

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 5

Can all L-ensemble distributions Pr(S)=det(L_S)/det(I+L), L⪰0, be represented exactly by polynomial-size decomposable probabilistic circuits when signed parameters are allowed? Likewise, does the uniform spanning-tree law on complete graphs admit such circuits?

**Context.** Doctoral researcher: Honghua Zhang. Dissertation: Tractable Probabilistic Inference via Probabilistic Circuits. University of California, Los Angeles, 2025. Primary advisor: Guy Van den Broeck. Attribution: These are the two explicitly unresolved determinantal-representation cases of the 2020 paper, repeated together in the 2025 dissertation. Shared setup for question 430: A decomposable circuit uses sums and products and has disjoint variable scopes at each product. It must compute the distribution’s network polynomial exactly. Signed weights are allowed internally. Smoothness can be added with polynomial overhead. An L-ensemble has a positive-semidefinite kernel; the spanning-tree law is a DPP but need not be an L-ensemble.

**Source.** Honghua Zhang; Steven Holtzen; Guy Van den Broeck. *On the Relationship Between Probabilistic Circuits and Determinantal Point Processes*. 2020. [primary source](https://www.auai.org/uai2020/proceedings/486_supp.pdf) · [dissertation](https://escholarship.org/content/qt5kd2v6p1/qt5kd2v6p1.pdf) · [record](https://escholarship.org/content/qt5kd2v6p1/qt5kd2v6p1.pdf) Location: §5.2 discussion in UAI 2020 extended source; Zhang thesis §5.4.2, printed p. 91.

**Literature check.** Status, checked 5 October 2026: Zhang’s 2025 thesis retains both cases. Polynomial unrestricted signed circuits proved in 2024 do not preserve decomposability.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.09085) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.02912) · [3](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v124/zhang20c.html)


<a id="q431"></a>

## Q431. Exact epistemic inference on binary polytrees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 5

What is the exact complexity under epistemic irrelevance for binary-variable polytrees? Is polynomial-time inference possible?

**Context.** Attribution: The 2014 complexity table leaves both inference regimes unresolved. Shared setup for questions 431, 432: A separately specified credal DAG has rational local conditional polytopes; their representation counts toward input size. Its strong extension is the convex hull of factorizing joint laws choosing local conditionals from these sets. Epistemic irrelevance instead constrains each node’s conditional law, also after conditioning on nondescendants, to its local set. Seek tight single-node posterior bounds given observed node values, requiring positive lower probability of the conditioning event.

**Source.** Denis Deratani Mauá; Cassio P. de Campos; Alessio Benavoli; Alessandro Antonucci. *Probabilistic Inference in Credal Networks: New Complexity Results*. 2014. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1613/jair.4355) Location: Complexity table and conclusion.

**Literature check.** Status, checked 5 October 2026: Latest exact open formulation located: 2014. Later credal-network and iHMM checks found no solution for binary-polytrees under epistemic irrelevance.

**Further links.** [1](https://ipg.idsia.ch/preprints/maua14jair.pdf) · [2](https://biblio.ugent.be/publication/6907551) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1701.08661)

## Q537. Exact nonadaptive evaluation of unit-cost k-of-n decisions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

With unit query costs and f(X)=1{∑X_i≥k}, is there an exact polynomial-time algorithm for the best fixed query permutation, stopping after k ones or n−k+1 zeros? The known PTAS does not settle exact optimization.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit residuals, including renewed older ordering questions; arbitrary-cost hardness does not establish unit-cost hardness. Shared setup for questions 537, 538, 539: Shared SBFE setup: independent Bernoulli inputs have known rational biases in (0,1). ## Q597. Completeness of countable probabilistic I/O rewriting

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 6

For an arbitrary I/O signature S, allowing operations with infinite response arities, consider well-founded programs built from requests Req k?(M_i) and countable probabilistic choice Σ\_n p_nM_n, with p_n≥0 and Σ\_np_n=1. Does equality of their probabilistic trace strategies imply equality in the least congruence generated by the ω-convex laws and commutation of each request with probabilistic choice? This is the tensor-completeness problem when both choice and the I/O signature are infinitary. Do not add a cancellation rule, under which completeness is already proved.

**Context.** Attribution: The authors isolate the jointly infinitary case after proving completeness when either component is finitary.

**Source.** Nathan Bowler; Sergey Goncharov; Paul Blain Levy. *Probabilistic Strategies: Definability and the Tensor Completeness Problem*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.09392) Location: Definition IV.2; Theorem IX.4; §X.

## Q616. Minimal square-root posterior uncertainty for balanced features

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.15) · **Collection** 7

For uniform X∈{−1,1}^n, balanced f:{−1,1}^n→{0,1}, and 0≤ρ≤1, define Tρf(x)=E[f(Y)|X=x] under independent coordinate noise of correlation ρ. Must E[sqrt(Tρf(X))]≥[sqrt((1+ρ)/2)+sqrt((1−ρ)/2)]/2? This is the asymmetric one-half-stability conjecture; its symmetric square-root counterpart is grouped with it, not counted separately.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit square-root endpoint conjecture; its symmetric consequence is grouped rather than counted separately.

**Source.** Lei Yu. *Local Optimality of Dictator Functions with Applications to Courtade–Kumar and Li–Médard Conjectures*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.10147) Location: 7 April 2026 v5, §5.3, Conjecture 5.15.

<a id="q636"></a>

## Q636. Does prequential credal randomness have a precise explanation?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

For rational credal polytopes C_n, is game-randomness of(C₁,x₁,…) equivalent to game-randomness of some compatible precise path(p₁,x₁,…), with rational p_n∈C_n? Neither forecast sequence need be computable.

**Context.** Doctoral researcher: Floris Persiau. Dissertation: Imprecise probabilities in algorithmic randomness. Ghent University, 2024. Supervision: Jasper De Bock (promoter); Gert de Cooman (promoter). Attribution: Explicit open questions in the cited sections. Shared setup for questions 636, 637: On finite X, φ(s)⊆Δ(X) is closed and convex; p(s)∈φ(s) means compatibility. CH-randomness requires limsup_n Σ\_{k&lt;n}S(x₁:k)[f(x_{k+1})−sup_{p∈φ(x₁:k)}E_p f]/Σ\_{k&lt;n}S(x₁:k)≤0 for every gamble f and computable selector choosing infinitely often; weak CH uses total selectors. Prequential game-randomness excludes zero-upper-probability outcomes and unbounded lower-semicomputable betting processes with initial capital 1, nonnegative capital, and nonincreasing expected capital under every announced forecast.

**Source.** Floris Persiau. *Imprecise Probabilities in Algorithmic Randomness*. 2024. Advisor(s): Jasper De Bock (promoter); Gert de Cooman (promoter). [primary source](https://users.ugent.be/~jdbock/documents/FP-2024-PhD-thesis.pdf) · [record](https://biblio.ugent.be/publication/01JFCWQGECYTFWE99GXFVM8VY9) Location: Conclusion, Future work pp. 204–205; Definitions 16.1,17.6 pp. 149,153; compare Theorem 20.1 and Corollary 20.3 pp. 193–194.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 636, 637, checked 5 October 2026: Checked 5 October 2026: no resolution located.

<a id="q638"></a>

## Q638. Do betting audits require a wider interval than frequency audits?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 7

Does there exist a binary path ω with I_CH(ω) strictly contained in I_C(ω)? Here I_R(ω) is the intersection of all closed probability intervals I⊆[0,1] for which ω is R-random; CH denotes Church randomness and C computable randomness. The CH intersection is attained, while C is interpreted through almost randomness: randomness for every strict enlargement [inf I−ε₁,sup I+ε₂]∩[0,1]. The known distinction between being C-random and merely almost C-random at the same interval is not a separation of the intervals.

**Context.** Attribution: The source explicitly leaves this target unresolved. Shared setup for question 638: For C-randomness, no nonnegative computable test supermartingale T, T(∅)=1, satisfying sup_{p∈I}[(1−p)T(s0)+pT(s1)]≤T(s) may be unbounded along ω. For CH-randomness, every recursively selected infinite subsequence has limiting lower/upper one-frequencies inside I.

**Source.** Floris Persiau; Gert de Cooman; Jasper De Bock. *A comparative study of the smallest probability intervals for which a binary sequence is random*. 2026. [primary source](https://users.ugent.be/~jdbock/documents/FP-2025-JCSS-paper.pdf) Location: §7 paragraph before §8, p. 20, Figure 4; Definitions 2–6; Proposition 5–6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 638, checked 5 October 2026: Checked 2026-10-05; no matched resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jcss.2025.103734) · [2](https://users.ugent.be/~jdbock/publications.html)

## Q733. Can negative association survive exchangeable joint representation?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

**Context.** Doctoral researcher: Liyuan Lin. Dissertation: Measures for risk, dependence and diversification. University of Waterloo, 2024. ## Q735. Does finite-menu representation commute with combination?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 8

For finite menus and arbitrary nonempty representing families 𝒟\_1,𝒟\_2, does γ(𝒟\_1⊗𝒟\_2)=cl_K(γ(𝒟\_1)∪γ(𝒟\_2))? The target is only this combination-commutation equality.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit finite-menu combination question; broader algebra claims are not used. Shared setup for question 735: Finite Ω=∏Ω\_i; gambles are real functions. A coherent cone D excludes 0, contains nonnegative nonzero gambles, and is closed under positive combinations. Let K_D={A:A∩D≠∅} for coherent D, K_L=all menus including∅, and γ(𝒟)=∩\_{D∈𝒟}K_D. Use the source’s finite-menu or infinitary coherence axioms as appropriate; cl_D and cl_K denote natural closure. Marginalization restricts coordinates. Combine families pointwise via cl_D(D_1∪D_2), and K models by cl_K(K_1∪K_2).

**Source.** Justyna Dąbrowska; Arthur Van Camp; Erik Quaeghebeur. *Sets of Desirable Gamble Sets and their Representations as Information Algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arthurvancamp.github.io/proceeding/smps2026/JD-SMPS2026-paper.pdf) Location: §4 p. 8; Proposition 11.

<a id="q738"></a>

## Q738. Do 3k moments uniquely identify a univariate Gaussian mixture?

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 15) · **Collection** 8

For arbitrary k, is the complexified moment map of k-component univariate Gaussian mixtures, with unknown means, variances and weights summing to 1, generically one-to-one modulo component permutation from moments m_1,…,m_{3k}? Equivalently, is the model rationally identifiable at order 3k? Algebraic finite-fiber identification at 3k−1 and rational identification at 3k+2 are established; these are not the target.

**Context.** Doctoral researcher: Carlos Enrique Améndola Cerón. Dissertation: Algebraic Statistics of Gaussian Mixtures. Technische Universität Berlin, 2017. Supervision: Bernd Sturmfels (supervisor); Christian Haase (co-supervisor). Attribution: Joint doctoral paper, later included in the thesis.

**Source.** Carlos Améndola; Jean-Charles Faugère; Bernd Sturmfels. *Moment Varieties of Gaussian Mixtures*. 2016. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1510.04654) · [dissertation](https://d-nb.info/1156013879/34) · [record](https://d-nb.info/1156013879/34) Location: arXiv Conjecture 15; thesis Conjecture 5.3.3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 738, checked 5 October 2026: Reaffirmed by Henriksson et al.(2025); the general 3k threshold remains unresolved in checked sources.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Generic rational identification of k univariate Gaussian components from moments 1..3k, modulo permutation, via an isolated-contact lemma and a non-weak-defectivity criterion. Scope: The stated complex generic target; not every exceptional parameter and not a stable recovery algorithm. Recorded from the external research packet ; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/packet-2026-10-05/bks_round4_research_report.md).
- **Earlier accepted result** (B7: prior accepted result): Imported accepted generic identification; new collision-stability obstruction. Remaining/limits: proved Intake: Prior accepted catalog result retained; returned collision-conditioning theorem is additional scope. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).
- **Related result** (B7-R10: submitted proved): For each fixed k>=2 and finite moment order d, positive k-component mixture pairs in all three families satisfy parameter-mixing-measure W1=epsilon and moment difference O(epsilon^(2k-1)). No uniform inverse Holder exponent above 1/(2k-1) is possible near collisions. Remaining/limits: No claim of optimal achievable exponent, separated-class stability, or sampling complexity. [Report](../../solutions/b7-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.15675) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.18421) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1137/23M1610082)

<a id="q743"></a>

## Q743. Can reduced information-bottleneck roots be singular away from bifurcations?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 8

For a finite information-bottleneck problem p(X,Y), write q_t(y)=p(y|t), w_t=p(t), and let BA_β denote the source’s Blahut–Arimoto map in their logarithmic coordinates. At every reduced fixed point for which log coordinates and the Jacobian are defined, is I−D_log(q),log(w)BA_β nonsingular except at bifurcation points? Reduced means all w_t>0 and pairwise distinct q_t. BA_β first sets r(t|x)∝w_t exp(−β KL(p(Y|x)||q_t)), then w′\_t=Σ\_xp(x)r(t|x), q′\_t(y)=Σ\_xp(x,y)r(t|x)/w′\_t, and returns (log q′,log w′).

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit question in the cited primary paper.

**Source.** Shlomi Agmon. *The Information Bottleneck’s Ordinary Differential Equation: First-Order Root Tracking for the Information Bottleneck*. 2023. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.3390/e25101370) Location: §5.1 Conjecture 1; preprint Conjecture 5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 743, checked 5 October 2026: No later resolution located; the reduced-root and defined-Jacobian restrictions are essential.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.09790) · [2](https://www.mdpi.com/1099-4300/25/10/1370) · [3](https://doi.org/10.3390/e24091231)


<a id="q812"></a>

## Q812. Does square-summable updating preserve a predictive density?

**Status:** Solved here: disproved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 9

Fix positive density f_0 on R, positive bivariate copula density c, and deterministic r_n∈(0, 1] with ∑r_n=∞, ∑r_n²<∞. Draw X_{n+1}∼f_n and update f_{n+1}(x)=f_n(x)[1−r_n+r_n c(F_n(x), F_n(X_{n+1}))], where F_n is the CDF. Must f_n(x)dx converge almost surely in total variation to its weak limit? No boundedness of c is assumed.

**Context.** Attribution: Explicit primary-source question.

**Source.** Samuele Garelli; Fabrizio Leisen; Luca Pratelli; Pietro Rigo. *Asymptotics of predictive distributions driven by sample means and variances*. 2026. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1017/jpr.2026.10119) Location: §2.3 after Theorem 4, equation(6).

**Literature check.** Status for question 812, checked 5 October 2026: Open in August 2026; bounded follow-up search checked 2026-10-05.

**Results.**

- **Disproved**: Answer no: a positive fixed symmetric (circular) copula with harmonic weights gives an almost-surely singular weak limit (R3). Positive results: TV convergence under a uniform conditional second-moment bound (S); complete Hellinger-series criterion for circular copulas; averaged Hellinger criterion, global L2 sufficiency under square-summable weights, finite-entropy sufficiency at harmonic rates for general kernels (R4). Scope: Universal assertion refuted; R3 also shows finite entropy alone is not enough for arbitrary square-summable schedules and no nonsummable schedule works uniformly. Recorded from the external research packet ; not independently re-derived here. [Details](../../solutions/packet-2026-10-05/01_solutions.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-applied-probability/article/asymptotics-of-predictive-distributions-driven-by-sample-means-and-variances/491E3396D1B3AEFB86ACEF1B54D0CCFA)

**Source.** Dmitry Chistikov; Stefan Kiefer; Ines Marušić; Mahsa Shirmohammadi; James Worrell. *Nonnegative Matrix Factorization Requires Irrationality*. 2017. [primary source](https://archive.model.in.tum.de/um/bibdb/kiefer/17CKMSW-SIAGA.pdf) Location: §5 pp. 304–305.

**Literature check.** Status for question 919, checked 5 October 2026: Shitov 2021 explicitly retains it, equivalent to rational Diophantine decidability; that remains open in 2026.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 10

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 10

For normalized U, is power universality equivalent to Φ(U)≠1 for every nondegenerate monotone homomorphism into ordinary or max-times nonnegative reals, with either order? Nondegenerate means nonconstant on comparable pairs.

Shared setup for question 928: For d≥2, Sres comprises finite nonnegative d-column matrices with nonempty common support (or zero), and prescribed pairwise support inclusions/equalities, modulo zero rows/permutations. <a id="q1032"></a>

## Q1032. A strict latent-simulation cost gap at negative Rényi orders

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 11

For sufficiently large β>0 and sufficiently small ε>0, must T̃\_{−β}(π)>1+h₂(ε)−2h₂(a)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1032: Author’s 2024 Conjecture 1; proposed numerical transition is unproved. Setup for question 1032: M_n is uniform on 2^{nR} symbols and X^n⊥Y^n|M_n. Write T̃\_α(π)=inf{R:D_α(P_n||π^{⊗n})/n→0}, where D_α(P||Q)=(α−1)^{-1}log₂ΣP^αQ^{1−α}. Let π=DSBS(ε), a=(1−√(1−2ε))/2, and h₂ be binary entropy.

**Source.** Lei Yu. *Rényi Common Information for Doubly Symmetric Binary Sources*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.06132) Location: Equation 19; definitions 7–8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1032, checked 6 October 2026: Current v1 checked; no resolution located through 2026-10-06.


<a id="q1033"></a>

## Q1033. Exact entropy cost of a binary-pair latent representation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 11

Determine lim_n G_α(π^{⊗n})/n for finite α>0, α≠1. Orders 0,1,∞ are excluded.

**Context.** Origin for question 1033: Authors’ 2022 open questions; exact-Rényi definition originated 2020. Setup for question 1033: For π=DSBS(ε), 0<ε<1/2, define G_α(π^{⊗n})=min H_α(W) over discrete W with X^n⊥Y^n|W and exact output law π^{⊗n}; H_α(W)=(1−α)^{-1}log₂ΣP(W=w)^α.

**Source.** Lei Yu; Vincent Y. F. Tan. *Common Information, Noise Stability, and Their Extensions*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.01788) Location: §11.1.2; equations7.14–7.15.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1033, checked 6 October 2026: No resolution located through 2026-10-06;2024 approximate-Rényi results have a different criterion.


<a id="q1034"></a>

## Q1034. Exact agreement frontier for a multibit common representation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 11

Determine max_f Pr[f(X)=f(Y)] uniformly in these parameters, for deterministic f:{0,1}^n→{0,1}^ℓ with uniform output.

**Context.** Origin for question 1034: Yu–Tan 2022, §11.2.1; follows Bogdanov–Mossel 2011. Setup for question 1034: For n≥ℓ≥3 and 0<ρ<1, let (X,Y) be n i.i.d. fair binary pairs of correlation ρ.

**Source.** Lei Yu; Vincent Y. F. Tan. *Common Information, Noise Stability, and Their Extensions*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.01788) Location: §11.2.1, equation 11.3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1034, checked 6 October 2026: Exact multibit frontier not located through 2026-10-06; asymptotic bounds do not settle it.


<a id="q1035"></a>

## Q1035. Can blockwise local sensors outperform all scalar decoders?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 11

For finite alphabets and zero Gács–Körner information, is simulability equivalent to existence of scalar kernels giving X−U−V−Y and the target p_XY?

**Context.** Origin for question 1035: Conjecture 1 in 2019 preprint, journal 2021. Setup for question 1035: Simulability means separate stochastic maps U^n→X^n,V^n→Y^n produce P_n with D(P_n||p_XY^{⊗n})→0 from i.i.d. p_UV, at unit rate, without communication or shared randomness. Zero Gács–Körner information means all common deterministic functions are constant.

**Source.** Tomer Berg; Ofer Shayevitz; Young-Han Kim; Lele Wang. *Distributed Source Simulation With No Communication*. 2021. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1906.06970) Location: Formulation equation 1; p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1035, checked 6 October 2026: No resolution located through 2026-10-06. One-pair finite-output simulation results do not settle unit-rate block generation.


<a id="q1036"></a>

## Q1036. Optimal balanced multiclass decoding of correlated Gaussian reports

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 11

Do these cells maximize Σ\_i Pr[X∈A_i,Y∈A_i] for all 0<ρ<1? Retain m=3,ρ>2/5 and general m≥4 as one frontier.

**Context.** Origin for question 1036: Mossel’s circa-2004 conjecture, stated by Isaksson–Mossel in 2009; journal 2012. Setup for question 1036: X,Y∈R^n are standard Gaussian with Cov(X,Y)=ρI. Partition into m equal-Gaussian-measure cells, 3≤m≤n+1. Regular-simplex cells maximize ⟨x,v_i⟩, with unit v_i and pairwise inner product−1/(m−1).

**Source.** Marcus Isaksson; Elchanan Mossel. *Maximally stable Gaussian partitions with discrete applications*. 2012. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/0903.3362) Location: Standard Simplex Conjecture; Heilman 2026 open directions.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1036, checked 6 October 2026: Heilman 2026 proves m=3,0<ρ≤2/5; remaining regimes explicitly open. Checked 2026-10-06.

Determine inf Pr[X∈A,Y∈B] over measurable sets of Gaussian measures μ,ν with A=−A. Requiring B=−B is equivalent.

**Context.** Origin for question 1037: Chakrabarti–Regev question, recorded explicitly by O’Donnell 2012. Setup for question 1037: X,Y are ρ-correlated standard Gaussian n-vectors; n≥2 and 0<ρ,μ,ν<1.

**Source.** Amit Chakrabarti; Oded Regev. *An optimal lower bound on the communication complexity of Gap-Hamming-Distance*. 2012. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1009.3460) Location: After Corollary 3.6, p.11; O’Donnell’s 2012 exact formulation.

<a id="q1038"></a>

## Q1038. Must optimal antisymmetric local agreement use a majority?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 11.1) · **Collection** 11

Must max_{f∈A_n}P_k(f)=max_{odd m≤n}P_k(Maj_m)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1038: O’Donnell’s Conjecture O; the paper presents Mossel’s competing conjecture too. Setup for question 1038: X is uniform on {−1,1}^n, n odd; k≥2 noisy views independently flip every bit with ε∈(0,1/2). A_n={f:{−1,1}^n→{−1,1}:f(−x)=−f(x)}. Write P_k(f)=Pr[f(Y¹)=⋯=f(Yᵏ)].

**Source.** Elchanan Mossel; Ryan O’Donnell. *Coin flipping from a cosmic source: On error correction of truly random bits*. 2005. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1002/rsa.20062) Location: Preprint p. 5; Yu–Tan 2022 Conjecture 11.1.

**Source.** Badih Ghazi; Pritish Kamath; Madhu Sudan. *Decidability of Non-Interactive Simulation of Joint Distributions*. 2016. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.04322) Location: Problem 2.2; §9.

## Q1185. Openness of random transient directions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.4.1) · **Collection** 12

For an iid random environment on Z^d, d≥2, with uniformly bounded jump range and almost surely irreducible quenched walk X starting at 0, is {ℓ∈S^{d−1}:X_n·ℓ→+∞} almost surely open in S^{d−1} under the annealed law?

**Context.** Origin for question 1185: Doctoral-research questions, also developed in the author’s papers.

**Source.** Daniel Slonim. *Random Walks in Dirichlet Environments with Bounded Jumps*. 2022. Advisor(s): Jonathon Peterson. [primary source](https://danielslonim.github.io/SlonimThesis.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.4.1, pp.68–69; assumptions (C1)–(C3), p.14.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1185, checked 6 October 2026: Directional conjecture reaffirmed in ALEA 2024, Conjecture 3.9; no subsequent resolution located.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B5: submitted unresolved): A restricted planar corollary is proved conditionally on the unverified ballisticity theorem: under iid uniform ellipticity, nearest-neighbor jumps, and an existing deterministic transient direction, the pathwise transient set is an open hemisphere. Remaining/limits: The full bounded-jump, pathwise random-set openness statement remains unresolved. Intake: Scope and identity checked; no independent proof or renewed literature audit of this question. [Report](../../solutions/b5-2026-10-07/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://alea.impa.br/articles/v21/21-27.pdf) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.14950)

## Q1186. Sharp transience on doubled trees

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.4) · **Collection** 12

If b>1+δ−δ/(3+2δ), must the walk have positive probability never to return to the root after its first step?

**Context.** Origin for question 1186: Thesis-origin conjecture. Setup for question 1186: Double every edge of an infinite locally finite rooted tree T. A walk starts at the root and chooses incident edges proportionally to weights: initially 1, then 1+δ after first traversal, δ>0. Put b=sup{s>0:inf_π Σ\_{v∈π}|v|^{−s}>0}, where π runs over vertex cutsets meeting every infinite root-ray and |v| is root-distance.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.2) · **Collection** 12

<a id="q1192"></a>

## Q1192. Monotonicity along the Kertész line

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.2) · **Collection** 12

For each q≥1, is h↦φ¹\_{p_c(q,h),q,h}(0↔∞) nonincreasing on [0,∞)?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1192: see question 1190. Origin for question 1192: Joint doctoral-research paper conjectures. Setup for question 1192: Fix d≥2. φ¹\_{p,q,h} is wired random-cluster measure on Z^d with cluster weight q>1, inner-edge parameter p and ghost-edge parameter 1−e^{−qh/(q−1)}. Connectivity excludes ghost edges. Let p_c(q,h)=inf{p:φ¹\_{p,q,h}(0↔∞)>0}; P(p,q,h) is infinite-volume pressure. At q=1 use Bernoulli percolation.

**Source.** Ulrik Thinggaard Hansen; Frederik Ravn Klausen. *Strict monotonicity, continuity and bounds on the Kertész line for the random-cluster model on Z^d*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.07033) Location: Conjecture 6.2, p.21.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1192, checked 6 October 2026: Checked 6 October 2026; no matching resolution found in the searches recorded.

<a id="q1193"></a>

## Q1193. Stochastic monotonicity of double currents

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 12

For every finite G, is the law of D_β stochastically nondecreasing in β? Equivalently, is Pr(D_β∈A) nondecreasing for every increasing edge-set event A?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1193: see question 1190. Origin for question 1193: Paper-origin monotonicity question; related set-connectivity question predates this paper. Setup for question 1193: For finite G and β>0, sample two independent currents n¹,n²∈N_0^{E(G)}, each with weight proportional to ∏\_e β^{n_e}/n_e! subject to even total incident current at each vertex. Let D_β={e:n¹\_e+n²\_e>0}.

**Source.** Frederik Ravn Klausen. *On monotonicity and couplings of random currents and the loop-O(1)-model*. 2022. Advisor(s): Albert H. Werner. [primary source](https://alea.impa.br/articles/v19/19-07.pdf) Location: Question 5.1, p.157.

<a id="q1194"></a>

## Q1194. Ends of percolating loop and single-current clusters

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.1) · **Collection** 12

On Z^d, d≥2, whenever either the loop-O(1) model or the single random current percolates, must its infinite cluster be one-ended?

**Context.** Origin for question 1194: Questions newly recorded in the July 2026 paper. Setup for question 1194: Loop-O(1) means the canonical limit of even edge sets weighted x^{|η|}, x∈(0,1]; the single current at β>0 is the canonical sourceless Ising current’s support. A connected infinite graph is one-ended when removal of any finite vertex set leaves exactly one infinite component. UEG is Haar probability on the compact group of all even subgraphs.

**Source.** Ulrik Thinggaard Hansen; Frederik Ravn Klausen. *The End of the Double Random Current*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07616) Location: Question 3.1, p.7.

## Q1195. Subcritical loop Gibbs uniqueness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.3) · **Collection** 12

For every 0&lt;x&lt;x_c, does the loop-O(1) specification on Z^d have a unique Gibbs measure, without assuming translation invariance?

**Context.** Origin for question 1195: Paper-origin question. Setup for question 1195: For d≥2, finite-volume loop-O(1) configurations have weight x^{|η|} and prescribed vertex parity. A Gibbs law on Z^d has these conditional distributions in every finite region, with parity dictated by exterior edges. Put x_c=tanh β\_c, the nearest-neighbor zero-field Ising critical point.

**Source.** Ulrik Thinggaard Hansen; Frederik Ravn Klausen. *The Supercritical Loop O(1) and Random Current models: Uniqueness and Mixing*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28520) Location: Question 1.3, p.3.

<a id="q1196"></a>

## Q1196. Planar hostile-environment coexistence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.2) · **Collection** 12

For d=2, do some p,λ give positive probability that type 1 and type 2 both survive?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1196, 1197: Joint doctoral-research paper questions. Setup for questions 1196, 1197: FPPHE on Z^d starts type 1 at 0 and iid dormant type-2 seeds of density p∈(0,1) elsewhere. Independent exponential edge passage times have rates 1 and λ>0. Types spread to vacant neighbors; hitting a seed activates type 2. Occupied vertices never change type. Type-1 survival means infinitely many occupied vertices; type-2 survival means an infinite connected component of its final occupied set.

**Source.** Thomas Finn; Alexandre Stauffer. *Non-equilibrium multi-scale analysis and coexistence in competing first passage percolation*. 2024. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.4171/JEMS/1309) Location: Question 6.2, preprint p.44.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1196, 1197, checked 6 October 2026: Checked 6 October 2026; no matching resolution found in the searches recorded.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.05463) · [2](https://researchportal.bath.ac.uk/en/studentTheses/topics-in-random-growth-models/)

<a id="q1198"></a>

## Q1198. Conversion-model survival phase diagram

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.4) · **Collection** 12

Does every λ<1 allow survival for sufficiently small ρ, and does some ρ\_c(d)∈(0,∞) separate survival for sufficiently small λ when ρ<ρ\_c from extinction for every λ>0 when ρ>ρ\_c?

**Context.** Doctoral context for question 1198: see question 1196. Origin for question 1198: Joint doctoral-research paper conjecture. Setup for question 1198: On Z^d, d≥2, start type 1 at 0 and all other sites vacant. Type 1 infects vacant neighbors at rate 1 per edge and converts irreversibly to type 2 at rate ρ>0 per site. Type 2 spreads to vacant or type-1 neighbors at rate λ>0 per edge. All clocks are independent; survival means type 1 stays nonempty forever.

**Source.** Thomas Finn; Alexandre Stauffer. *Coexistence in competing first passage percolation with conversion*. 2022. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1214/22-AAP1792) Location: Conjecture 1.4, preprint p.3.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.4) · **Collection** 12

Do constants C_{r,p},E_{r,p}<∞ exist such that (E||Σ\_{i=1}^n ε\_iT_i||\_{I_p}²)^{1/2}≤C_{r,p}d^{1/2−1/p}(log(2d))^{E_{r,p}}(Σ\_i||T_i||\_{I_p}²)^{1/2} for all d,n≥1 and T_i∈(R^d)^{⊗r}, with independent uniform signs ε\_i?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 12

## Q1287. Order of the quenched Ising transition

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

For the ferromagnetic Ising law proportional to exp(βΣ\_{uv∈E}σ\_uσ\_v+BΣ\_vσ\_v), determine the order of the zero-field transition: the asymptotics of M(β)=lim_{B↓0}lim_{n→∞}n⁻¹Σ\_v⟨σ\_v⟩ as β↓β\_c(m,δ). Here β\_c=inf{β:M(β)>0}. The n→∞ limit is in probability over the attachment graph.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1287, 1288: Doctoral discussion; later Ising paper identifies the critical point, not its order. Setup for questions 1287, 1288: Use thesis model (d): start with two vertices joined by m parallel edges; each new vertex adds m edges sequentially to older vertices, choosing proportionally to current degree plus δ. Here m≥2 and δ>−m.

## Q1289. Critical universality of spread-out discrete Gaussian fields

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.2.2) · **Collection** 13

For all sufficiently large ρ, does the torus Gaussian-free-field scaling limit hold down to β=β\_c(ρ), with effective temperature β\_eff(J_ρ,β\_c)=8πv_{J_ρ}² and β\_c(ρ)∼(4π/3)ρ² as ρ→∞? The limit covariance is β\_eff/v_J² times the inverse negative Laplacian on the unit two-torus, on mean-zero test functions.

**Context.** Origin for question 1289: Thesis Conjecture 1.2.2 extends the author’s joint high-temperature work. Setup for question 1289: Let J_ρ={x∈Z²:0<‖x‖∞≤ρ}, Δ\_J f(x)=|J|⁻¹Σ\_{y∈J}(f(x+y)−f(x)), and v_J²=(2|J|)⁻¹Σ\_{x∈J}x₁². The pinned torus field σ∈2πZ has law proportional to exp[−(σ,−Δ\_Jσ)/(2β)]; β is temperature.

**Source.** Jiwoon Park. *Two-dimensional Discrete Gaussian model at high temperature*. 2023. Advisor(s): Roland Bauerschmidt. [primary source](https://api.repository.cam.ac.uk/server/api/core/bitstreams/dea95369-e6f9-4874-9761-5d3b642bfeba/content) Location: Conjecture 1.2.2, p.12; Theorem 1.1.1.

<a id="q1290"></a>

## Q1290. Separation cutoff for positively biased shuffles

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.7.9) · **Collection** 13

For every fixed α>0 with α≠1, does this walk on S_n have separation-distance cutoff at t_n,α log n, as n→∞?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1290, 1291: Thesis conjectures; unbiased cases and branching rules have since been proved. Setup for questions 1290, 1291: For fixed α∈R, put S_a(n)=Σ\_{j=1}^n j^a and t_n,α=S_α(n)/n^α if α≤1, otherwise S_α(n)/S_{α−1}(n). Each shuffle chooses j with probability j^α/S_α(n), i uniformly in {1,…,j}, and transposes i,j.

**Source.** Oliver Matheau-Raven. *Random walks on the symmetric group: cutoff for one-sided transposition shuffles*. 2020. Advisor(s): Stephen Connor and Michael Bate. [primary source](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/28076/1/OMatheauRavenThesis.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.7.9, p.103.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1290, 1291, checked 6 October 2026: Biased targets remain open in Deng–Tan (2024 revision); no later resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Separation cutoff proved for every fixed 0&lt;alpha<1; alpha>1 remains unresolved. Scope: Every fixed alpha<=1; this settles the requested 0&lt;alpha<1 range. Assumptions: Restrict the original positive-alpha question to 0&lt;alpha<1; the proof also covers the excluded endpoint and nonpositive biases. Restrictions/extensions: Every fixed alpha<=1; this settles the requested 0&lt;alpha<1 range. Remaining: The interval alpha>1; no optimal additive separation window is established. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.10462) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.05085)

## Q1291. Total-variation cutoff for biased signed shuffles

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.4.4) · **Collection** 13

On signed permutations B_n, follow each transposition by a fair coin: on tails flip both selected cards, or flip the single card once if i=j. For every fixed nonzero α∈R, is there total-variation cutoff at t_n,α log n?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1290, 1291: Thesis conjectures; unbiased cases and branching rules have since been proved. Setup for questions 1290, 1291: For fixed α∈R, put S_a(n)=Σ\_{j=1}^n j^a and t_n,α=S_α(n)/n^α if α≤1, otherwise S_α(n)/S_{α−1}(n). Each shuffle chooses j with probability j^α/S_α(n), i uniformly in {1,…,j}, and transposes i,j.

**Source.** Oliver Matheau-Raven. *Random walks on the symmetric group: cutoff for one-sided transposition shuffles*. 2020. Advisor(s): Stephen Connor and Michael Bate. [primary source](https://etheses.whiterose.ac.uk/id/eprint/28076/1/OMatheauRavenThesis.pdf) Location: Conjecture 4.4.4, pp.130–131.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1290, 1291, checked 6 October 2026: Biased targets remain open in Deng–Tan (2024 revision); no later resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Total-variation cutoff at tau_n log n for every fixed real alpha, including the full requested nonzero range. Scope: Full original statement, with alpha=0 also included. Assumptions: Alpha is a fixed real number independent of n; diagonal selections flip one sign once. Remaining: Nothing remains at the original cutoff scope; an optimal window or full profile is not asserted. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.10462) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.05085)

<a id="q1295"></a>

## Q1295. Distinct speeds for surviving annihilating blocks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 13

On {K=k}, do the k surviving interfaces have almost-sure limits I_j(t)/t as t→∞, with all limiting speeds pairwise distinct?

**Context.** Origin for question 1295: Question arising from the authors’ coexistence and two-block speed results. Setup for question 1295: Start finitely many Brownian particles on R with m≥2 types, no different types at the same point. Particles split dyadically at rate one; different types annihilate upon meeting. A block is a maximal same-type segment; interfaces are midpoints of the gaps between adjacent blocks. Their number decreases to K.

**Source.** Daniel Ahlberg, Omer Angel and Brett Kolesnik. *Annihilating Branching Brownian Motion*. 2024. [primary source](https://academic.oup.com/imrn/article/2024/13/10425/7667245) Location: §6.2, Question 4 and following paragraph; preprint Question 20.

<a id="q1296"></a>

## Q1296. Monotonicity under branching, deletion and merging

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

Is P(Z_n>0 for every n) nondecreasing in p and nonincreasing in q?

**Context.** Origin for question 1296: Primary-paper question; no dissertation origin asserted. Setup for question 1296: For p>−1,q∈[0,1], start one root. A last-generation vertex u has independent Poisson((1+p)(1−q)^k_u) children, where k_u counts existing vertices at graph distance three. After adding children, independently mark each pair of new vertices at distance four with probability q; merge each equivalence class generated by marked pairs, uniting incident edges and removing duplicates. Z_n is generation size.

**Source.** Laura Eslava, Sarah Penington and Fiona Skerman. *A branching process with deletions and mergers that matches the threshold for hypercube percolation*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2104.04407) Location: After Theorem 1.1 and §2.3.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1296, checked 6 October 2026: Reaffirmed in the 2024 journal version; no subsequent resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B6: unresolved): No monotonicity proof or counterexample obtained. Remaining/limits: A coupling must respect state-dependent suppression and merger equivalence classes. [Report](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://purehost.bath.ac.uk/ws/portalfiles/portal/261835462/Branching_with_cousin_mergers_AIHP_v2.pdf) · [2](https://sigma.iimas.unam.mx/laura/research.html)

## Q1297. Sharp barely-supercritical giant normalization

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.6) · **Collection** 13

Does there exist c=c(γ)>0 such that, for every β\_n→β\_c with liminf a_n>π², L_n/[n exp{−π/(2√(4β\_c(β\_n−β\_c)))}]→c in probability?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1297, 1298: New primary-paper conjecture and secondary-window question. Setup for questions 1297, 1298: Fix γ∈[0,1/2). On [n], edges i&lt;j are independently present with probability min{β\_n i^(−γ)j^(γ−1),1}. Set β\_c=1/4−γ/2, a_n=4β\_c(β\_n−β\_c)(log n)², and L_n=largest-component cardinality.

**Source.** Joost Jorritsma, Pascal Maillard and Peter Mörters. *The critical percolation window in growing random graphs*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.18937) Location: Conjecture 2.6, p.5.

## Q1299. Intermediate loop phase on a lattice

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.3) · **Collection** 13

Does a periodic lattice L admit 0&lt;x_0&lt;a&lt;b&lt;x_1<1 such that ℓ\_{x_0,L} has exponential connectivity decay, ℓ\_{x_1,L} percolates, and neither occurs for every x∈(a,b)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1299: Doctoral joint-paper conjectures, retained in the 2025 revision. Setup for question 1299: ℓ\_x is the canonical infinite-volume loop-O(1) law: finite even edge sets have weight x^{|η|}. C_0 is the origin cluster; x_c=tanh β\_c for the zero-field nearest-neighbor Ising threshold.

**Source.** Ulrik Thinggaard Hansen; Boris Kjær; Frederik Ravn Klausen. *The Uniform Even Subgraph and Its Connection to Phase Transitions of Graphical Representations of the Ising Model*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05130) Location: Question 6.3, p.47.

## Q1300. Even-subgraph percolation from transience

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.3) · **Collection** 13

Does the UEG of every connected locally finite unimodular transient graph have an infinite component almost surely?

**Context.** Origin for question 1300: Questions newly recorded in the July 2026 paper. Setup for question 1300: Loop-O(1) means the canonical limit of even edge sets weighted x^{|η|}, x∈(0,1]; the single current at β>0 is the canonical sourceless Ising current’s support. A connected infinite graph is one-ended when removal of any finite vertex set leaves exactly one infinite component. UEG is Haar probability on the compact group of all even subgraphs.

**Source.** Ulrik Thinggaard Hansen; Frederik Ravn Klausen. *The End of the Double Random Current*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07616) Location: Question 3.3, p.7.

## Q1384. A monotone recurrence boundary for lattice frogs

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 14

For every d≥2, is there a nonincreasing f_d:[0,1]→[0,1] such that the model is recurrent when w&lt;f_d(α) and transient when w>f_d(α)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1384: Joint doctoral research; thesis Conjecture 1.18 restates the paper. Setup for question 1384: On Z^d, start one active frog at 0 and one sleeping frog elsewhere. Activated frogs independently make discrete-time nearest-neighbor walks, waking sleeping frogs they visit. For w,α∈[0,1], jumps ±e₁ have probabilities w(1±α)/2 and each ±e_j, j≥2, has probability (1−w)/(2(d−1)). Recurrence means infinitely many visits to 0 almost surely.

**Source.** Christian Döbler, Nina Gantert, Thomas Höfelsauer, Serguei Popov, Felizitas Weidner. *Recurrence and Transience of Frogs with Drift on Z^d*. 2018. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.00038) Location: Paper Conjecture 4.1, pp.20–21; thesis Conjecture 1.18, p. 14.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1384, checked 6 October 2026: Checked 6 October 2026: v2 and subsequent searches leave this boundary unresolved.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.1) · **Collection** 14

## Q1387. Monotonicity without frog deaths

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

For each d≥2, is the recurrence probability nondecreasing in p on 0&lt;p<1/2?

**Context.** Origin for question 1387: Original paper question; p≥1/2 is already recurrent by the initial frog alone. Setup for question 1387: On the infinite rooted d-ary tree, d≥2, start one active frog at the root and one sleeper elsewhere. Active frogs independently walk forever, waking visited sleepers. Away from the root they move to the parent with probability p and to each child with probability (1−p)/d. At the root they always choose a uniform child. Recurrence means infinitely many total root visits.

**Source.** Erin Beckman, Natalie Frank, Yufeng Jiang, Matthew Junge, Si Tang. *The frog model on trees with drift*. 2019. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1808.03283) Location: Introduction, pp.2–3; model definition, p. 1.

## Q1388. A single rotor bias threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 14

If each initial arrow points west with probability p and each other direction with probability (1−p)/3, is there a single p_c separating almost-sure recurrence for p&lt;p_c from transience for p>p_c?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1388, 1389, 1390: Paper §7; the disk prediction is attributed to Kapri–Dhar 2009. Setup for questions 1388, 1389, 1390: A rotor walk starts at 0; each step advances the current vertex’s arrow in a fixed cyclic order and follows it. Initial arrows are independent. On Z² the order is clockwise; with uniform arrows the visited set R_t, scaled by t^(−1/3), has deterministic convex limit B.

**Source.** Ahmed Bou-Rabee; Yuval Peres. *Eulerian walkers on Z^2 have range exponent 2/3*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.23545) Location: §7, Problem 2, pp.25–26.
