## Q1393. Delayed epidemic extinction below static percolation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question A) · **Collection** 14

For every λ<λ\_c, is the delayed model’s critical removal rate α\_c(λ) finite?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1393, 1394, 1395: Source-stated questions A–C. Setup for questions 1393, 1394, 1395: In R^d, d≥2, a Poisson population of intensity λ plus one initially infected particle at 0 performs independent standard Brownian motions. Infection transmits instantly at distance≤1; infectives are independently removed forever after Exp(α) times. In the delayed model, susceptibles remain fixed until infected; otherwise everyone moves from time 0. Let θ(λ,α) be the probability infinitely many particles are infected and λ\_c the percolation threshold for the union of closed unit balls centered at Poisson points.

**Source.** Geoffrey R. Grimmett and Zhongyang Li. *Brownian snails with removal: epidemics in diffusing populations*. 2022. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.02495) Location: §1.4, Question A, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1393, 1394, 1395, checked 6 October 2026: Checked 6 October 2026: no matching resolution found. Hartarsky–Lichev resolves only the separate one-dimensional question.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.10751) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1214/22-EJP804)

## Q1396. Monotonicity of conversion survival

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

Is the type-1 survival probability nonincreasing in each of λ and ρ?

**Context.** Origin for question 1396: Joint doctoral-research paper conjecture and question. Setup for question 1396: On Z^d, d≥2, start type 1 at 0 and all other sites vacant. Type 1 infects vacant neighbors at rate 1 per edge and converts irreversibly to type 2 at rate ρ>0 per site. Type 2 spreads to vacant or type-1 neighbors at rate λ>0 per edge. All clocks are independent; survival means type 1 stays nonempty forever.

**Source.** Thomas Finn; Alexandre Stauffer. *Coexistence in competing first passage percolation with conversion*. 2022. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1214/22-AAP1792) Location: Introduction, preprint p. 2.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

For π\_n=π\_c+b/(log n)² with fixed b∈R, determine the asymptotic scales and limiting laws of the largest component sizes in the fixed-two-outedge model.

## Q1399. Openness of pressure discontinuity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.1) · **Collection** 14

**Context.** Origin for question 1399: Joint doctoral-research paper conjectures. Setup for question 1399: Fix d≥2. φ¹\_{p,q,h} is wired random-cluster measure on Z^d with cluster weight q>1, inner-edge parameter p and ghost-edge parameter 1−e^{−qh/(q−1)}. <a id="q1400"></a>

## Q1400. Survival with general host immunities

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 14

Which distributions of I and A yield positive probability that parasites remain alive at every time?

**Context.** Origin for question 1400: Thesis-origin question. Setup for question 1400: In the discrete-time SIMI on Z^d, d≥2, parasites independently take simple-random-walk steps. Each uninfected site has iid immunity I∈N∪{∞}. Its first I−1 attacks kill the attacker; attack I kills host and attacker and creates an independent A∈N_0 offspring, E[A]<∞. All immunity, offspring and walk randomness are independent. Start with A_0 parasites at an emptied origin and hosts elsewhere, where A_0 is an independent copy of A.

**Source.** Sascha Josef Franck. *Survival and parasite spread in a spatial host-parasite model with host immunity*. 2025. Advisor(s): Cornelia Pokalyuk. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1367705088/34) Location: Chapter 6, Open Problem 2, p. 191; model §§1.1,3.2.1.

## Q1488. Strict convexity of the passage-time shape

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4) · **Collection** 15

Must B have no nontrivial line segment in its boundary?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1488, 1489, 1490, 1491: Classical problems restated here. Setup for questions 1488, 1489, 1490, 1491: For shape/geodesic questions, edges of Z² have iid nonnegative weights τ\_e with continuous distribution and Eτ\_e^(2+ε)<∞ for some ε>0. T(x,y) minimizes path-weight sums, Γ(x,y) is the unique minimizer, and B is the deterministic limit shape.

## Q1492. Infinite interchange cycles in lower dimensions

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

## Q1495. Full high-temperature SK Glauber mixing

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 15) · **Collection** 15

For each fixed β∈(0,1) and arbitrary deterministic external fields h=h_n∈R^n, is the worst-start total-variation mixing time of SK Glauber dynamics to error 1/4 polynomial in n, with high probability over J?

Setup for question 1495: Let J_ij=J_ji be independent N(0,β²/n) for i&lt;j, J_ii=0, and μ(x)∝exp(xᵀJx/2+hᵀx), x∈{±1}^n. Each discrete Glauber step refreshes one uniformly chosen spin from its conditional Gibbs law.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

## Q1587. Gated and one-limited polling law

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 16

For two queues with independent Poisson arrivals, gated service at Q1 and 1-limited service at Q2, determine the joint stationary queue-length probability generating functions at server-arrival epochs, for general service and switch-over distributions.

**Context.** Doctoral context for questions 1587, 1588: Marcus Aloysius Antonius Boon. Polling Models: From Theory to Traffic Intersections. PhD, Eindhoven University of Technology, 2011. Advisors: Onno J. Boxma; Ivo J. B. F. Adan. Origin for questions 1587, 1588: Joint 2011 questions; thesis restatements. Setup for questions 1587, 1588: A cyclic server visits independent queues. Service times are iid within each queue; all arrival, service and switch-over processes are independent. Assume stationarity. A branching-type discipline independently replaces each visit-start customer by a random population with fixed multivariate PGF in the Poisson-input reference system. Gated service handles exactly customers present at arrival; 1-limited service handles at most one.

**Source.** Marko Boon, Onno Boxma, Erik Winands. *On open problems in polling systems*. 2011. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1408.0142) Location: Open Problem 1, §3.2, p. 4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1587, 1588, checked 6 October 2026: no matching general solution found.

<a id="q1589"></a>

## Q1589. Nonequilibrium exclusion cutoff profile

**Status:** Solved here: disproved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 16

For the rate-N² segment SSEP, fix p, q∈(0, 1), q≤min{p, 1−p}, p≠q. Put S(x)=Σ\_u x(u), t_N(b)=(log N)/(2π²)+b/π², and let π\_N be stationary. Does there exist f_pq>0 with (E_1 S(X_{t_N(b)})−E_{π\_N}S)/√Var_{π\_N}S→f_pq e^(−b) and worst-case TV distance at t_N(b) tending to TV(N(0, 1), N(f_pq e^(−b), 1)) for every b∈R?

**Context.** Origin for question 1589: Thesis-proposed extensions of its reservoir-exclusion results. Setup for question 1589: The segment is {1, …, N}. Each adjacent pair swaps occupations at rate N²; site 1 refreshes to Bernoulli(p) and site N to Bernoulli(q), each at rate N².

**Source.** Hong-Quan Tran. *Cutoff phenomenon for some interacting particle systems*. 2024. Advisor(s): Justin Salez. [primary source](https://www.unige.ch/~tranh/these-version-240521.pdf) Location: Chapter 2, Conjecture 2 and (2.7)–(2.8), p. 20.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1589, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution found; Chen’s general-profile proof was withdrawn 9 September 2026.

**Results.**

- **Disproved**: The proposed unweighted-count Gaussian profile is false: p=1/2, q=1/4 gives a strictly larger sine-statistic TV lower bound. Scope: The specified universal Gaussian profile with shift fixed by unweighted S. Assumptions: Exact permitted densities and the original boundary and N^2-rate normalization. Remaining: The original formula is refuted; the correct nonequilibrium profile and a matching upper bound are not determined. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.03685) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.14687)

## Q1590. Noncrossing matching in a strip

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2) · **Collection** 16

**Context.** Origin for questions 1590, 1591: Plane question: Yuval Peres 2002; variants: Holroyd 2009. Setup for questions 1590, 1591: R and B are independent unit-intensity Poisson processes. ## Q1594. Higher-dimensional perfectness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 16

For each d≥2 and each finite γ, is it almost surely true that every γ-minimal matching is perfect, in both one- and two-color models?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1594, 1595, 1596: Source-explicit remaining cases. Setup for questions 1594, 1595, 1596: Use unit-intensity Poisson points in R^d; for two colors use independent copies and red–blue edges. On every finite collection of edges and unmatched points, γ-minimality first minimizes unmatched count, then total cost cγ(r)=r^γ (γ>0), −r^γ (γ<0), log r (γ=0). No invariance is imposed.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 1) · **Collection** 16

**Context.** Origin for question 1600: Source Problem 1 and its explicitly highlighted power-step case. Setup for question 1600: Let ε\_k be independent uniform signs and X_n=Σ\_{k=1}^n ε\_k k^α. Weak recurrence means P(∃C<∞:|X_n|≤C infinitely often)=1; transience means this probability is 0.

## Q1684. A sharp SERPT mean-delay bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

For every such size distribution and load, is E[T_SERPT]≤2E[T_Gittins]?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1684, 1685: M-SERPT: Scully–Harchol-Balter–Scheller-Wolf (2020), Conjecture 8.1; thesis also conjectures SERPT’s sharp factor. Setup for questions 1684, 1685: Consider a stationary preempt-resume M/G/1 of unit speed, Poisson rate λ and iid nonnegative sizes S of known distribution but unknown realizations, with ρ=λE[S]<1. A job’s age a is attained service. SERPT serves minimum rank r(a)=E[S−a|S>a]; M-SERPT uses sup_{0≤b≤a}r(b). Equal ranks use arrival order. T_π is stationary response time; Gittins is the optimal nonanticipating policy with this information.

**Source.** Ziv Scully. *A New Toolbox for Scheduling Theory*. 2022. Advisor(s): Mor Harchol-Balter and Guy Blelloch. [primary source](https://www.csd.cmu.edu/sites/default/files/phd-thesis/CMU-CS-22-132.pdf) Location: §11.3, p. 151.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1684, 1685, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution found; heavy-traffic factor-two results do not prove these all-load bounds.

<a id="q1692"></a>

## Q1692. Euclidean hard-sphere nonuniqueness

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

For each d∈{2,3}, does some finite λ>0 admit at least two distinct radius-one hard-sphere Gibbs laws on R^d?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1692, 1693: Euclidean phase question is classical; convergence question is source Question 68. Setup for questions 1692, 1693: A radius-r hard-sphere Gibbs law on X is supported on point sets with mutual distances ≥2r. Conditional on points outside each bounded region, points inside follow a rate-λ Poisson process conditioned on mutual separation and separation from outside points. In empty-start birth–death dynamics, proposed points arrive at intensity λ per unit volume and time, are accepted only at distance ≥2r from existing points, and die after independent Exp(1) lifetimes.

**Source.** Lewis Bowen, Marcus Michelen, Will Perkins. *A phase transition for the hard sphere model on the hyperbolic plane*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23873) Location: Introduction and Definition 1, pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1692, 1693, checked 6 October 2026: July 2026 primary preprint leaves these open; its theorem concerns hyperbolic nonuniqueness and subsequential limits.

## Q1698. Exact fractional-integral persistence exponent

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

Determine θ\_R(H) for H>0 outside the known cases H=1/2 and H=3/2.

**Context.** Origin for question 1698: Classical Riemann–Liouville persistence problem, restated in thesis §1.3. Setup for question 1698: Let W be standard Brownian motion and R_H(t)=∫\_0^t(t−s)^(H−1/2)dW_s for H>0. Define θ\_R(H)=−lim_{T→∞}log P(sup_{0≤t≤T}R_H(t)≤1)/log T; this limit exists.

**Source.** Martin Alexander Dennis Kilian. *Persistence problems for fractional processes*. 2022. Advisor(s): Frank Aurzada. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1277862419/34) Location: §1.3, p. 9; Chapter3, §3.1.

<a id="q1699"></a>

## Q1699. Giant size from Erlang-tree survival

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 17

Does E[|C_max(G_n)|]/n→P(|T|=∞), where C_max is a largest connected component?

**Context.** Origin for question 1699: Source §4, Problem 3; finite model originates with La–Kabkab (2015). Setup for question 1699: Let P be a law on positive integers with finite mean and P(1)<1. On K_n draw iid D_i∼P and independent Exp(rate 1/n) edge costs; retain an edge exactly when both endpoints rank it among their min(D_i,n−1) cheapest. Construct a backbone with D potential children at its root and D−1 at each other vertex, using independent D∼P. A vertex with m potential children has V∼Gamma(m+1,1); its outgoing costs are iid Uniform[0,V]. Keep parent–child edges only when their cost is below the child’s V, and let T be the root component, the Erlang weighted tree.

**Source.** Mehrdad Moharrami, Vijay Subramanian, Mingyan Liu, Rajesh Sundaresan. *The Erlang weighted tree, a new branching process*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2002.03993) Location: §2.3 and §4, Problem 3, pp. 10,16 (arXiv).

**Literature check.** Status for question 1699, checked 6 October 2026: no matching giant-size theorem found; growing-degree connectivity results differ.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1002/rsa.21180) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05677)

## Q1787. Higher-dimensional critical-time cutoff

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

On V_L=Z^d∩B(0,L), add a particle uniformly and stabilize each step, killing exits. Writing N_L for the stationary particle count, does some ρ\_s>0 satisfy N_L/|V_L|→ρ\_s in probability and t_mix(ε)/|V_L|→ρ\_s for every ε∈(0,1), with worst-case total-variation mixing?

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

For which p∈(0,1), if any, is there almost surely an infinite trajectory?

**Context.** Origin for question 1794: Classical unweighted Lorentz mirror problem; source §1.2 restates it. Setup for question 1794: Independently at vertices of Z² place no mirror with probability p∈(0,1), or either 45-degree mirror orientation with probability (1−p)/2. A ray follows lattice edges, reflects at mirrors and continues straight at empty vertices.

**Source.** Kieran Ryan. *The spin-1/2 Heisenberg XXZ chain and the Lorentz mirror model with loop weight 2*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21306) Location: §1.2, opening paragraphs.

## Q1795. Optimal second-moment queue scheduling

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

Which scheduling policy minimizes E[T²] for each such workload?

**Context.** Origin for question 1795: Thesis §8.3.5 explicitly asks for optimal second-moment scheduling. Setup for question 1795: Take a stationary M/G/1 with independent Poisson arrivals and iid job sizes whose distribution and arriving realizations are known. T denotes time from arrival to completion. Scheduling is nonanticipating and may preempt and resume without cost; consider stable workloads for which the objective is finite.

**Source.** Isaac Grosof. *Optimal Scheduling in Multiserver Queues*. 2023. Advisor(s): Mor Harchol-Balter. [primary source](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~harchol/isaac-thesis.pdf) Location: §8.3.5, pp. 283–284.

## Q1800. Altruistic Poisson matching existence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

For each d≥2 and two colors, does a perfect ∞-minimal matching almost surely exist?

**Context.** Origin for question 1800: Source-explicit remaining cases. Setup for question 1800: Let red and blue points be independent unit-intensity Poisson processes in R^d. A matching is ∞-minimal if every finite rearrangement first minimizes the number of unmatched points, then lexicographically minimizes its decreasing list of edge lengths. No invariance is required.

**Source.** Alexander Holroyd, Svante Janson, Johan Wästlund. *Minimal matchings of point processes*. 2022. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00440-022-01151-y) Location: After Theorem 7; §2.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1800, checked 6 October 2026: no matching resolution found; 2025 strip progress differs.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.07129) · [2](https://www.bristolmathsresearch.org/seminar/luke-turvey-2/)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

**Context.** Origin for question 1884: Joint work with Zheneng Xie, preprint 2021; thesis §3.6(2). Sample n iid degree pairs conditioned on equal totals and graphicality, then a uniform simple digraph G_n with those degrees. Diam(G_n) is the largest finite directed distance.

<a id="q1885"></a>

## Q1885. Universal BST lower bound

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.2.2) · **Collection** 19

For every μ and ε>0, is P(h(T_n)<(c_\*−ε)log n)→0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1885, 1886: Corsini–Dubach–Féray, Binary search trees of permuton samples (2024/2025). Setup for questions 1885, 1886: A permuton μ is a probability measure on [0,1]² with uniform marginals. From n iid μ-points, sort by x and insert their y-values into a binary search tree T_n. Let h denote graph height and c_\*≥2 solve c_\*log(2e/c_\*)=1.

**Source.** Victor Dubach. *On Some Models of Non-Uniform Random Permutations*. 2025. Advisor(s): Valentin Féray. [primary source](https://docnum.univ-lorraine.fr/public/DDOC_T_2025_0061_DUBACH.pdf) Location: Conjecture 3.2.2, p. 124.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1885, 1886, checked 6 October 2026: thesis leaves both open; no later resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: Almost-sure liminf H_n/log n >= c_\* if mu has continuous positive density on any nondegenerate rectangle touching the left edge; no condition outside that rectangle. Scope: Arbitrary permuton mu (uniform marginals); sort iid mu-points by x and insert y-values into a BST; graph height and c_\*>2 satisfying c_\* log(2e/c_\*)=1. Restrictions/extensions: For the partial theorem only: restrict mu to a continuous positive density on one nondegenerate left-edge rectangle; outside it mu may be singular. Convergence is strengthened to an almost-sure liminf in the nested iid coupling. Remaining: The universal arbitrary-permuton lower bound, including singular cases, remains. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/B6_Research_Report_2026-10-08.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.03151) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aam.2024.102774) · [3](https://doctorat.univ-lorraine.fr/sites/default/files/user/ecoles-doctorales/IAEM/Conseil_ED_IAEM_23_06_2022.pdf)

## Q1886. Almost-sure sampled BST height

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Use the first n points of one infinite iid μ-sequence. If μ has bounded density, continuous and positive near {0}×[0,1], does h(T_n)/log n→c_\* almost surely?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1885, 1886: Corsini–Dubach–Féray, Binary search trees of permuton samples (2024/2025). Setup for questions 1885, 1886: A permuton μ is a probability measure on [0,1]² with uniform marginals. From n iid μ-points, sort by x and insert their y-values into a binary search tree T_n. Let h denote graph height and c_\*≥2 solve c_\*log(2e/c_\*)=1.

**Source.** Victor Dubach. *On Some Models of Non-Uniform Random Permutations*. 2025. Advisor(s): Valentin Féray. [primary source](https://docnum.univ-lorraine.fr/public/DDOC_T_2025_0061_DUBACH.pdf) Location: Remark 17, pp. 124–125.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1885, 1886, checked 6 October 2026: thesis leaves both open; no later resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: H_n/log n -> c_\* almost surely in the exact nested iid planar-sample coupling under the exact retained density hypotheses. Polynomial marginal deviation bounds are also proved. Scope: The first n points of one infinite iid permuton sequence, ordered by x and inserted by y; mu has globally bounded density, continuous and positive on a neighborhood of the entire edge {0}x[0,1]. <a id="q1887"></a>

## Q1887. Uniform limit with many internals

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.1.4) · **Collection** 19

Whenever n,k→∞ and k/n→∞, does (n+k)⁻¹∑\_{i=1}^{n+k}δ\_{(i/(n+k),σ\_{n,k}(i)/(n+k))} converge weakly in probability to Lebesgue measure on [0,1]²?

**Context.** Origin for question 1887: Borga–Duchi–Slivken (2019/2021), sharpened in thesis Conjecture 5.1.4(e). Setup for question 1887: In a permutation, an external entry is a minimum or maximum among all entries to its left or among all entries to its right; remaining entries are internal. Let σ\_{n,k} be uniform among size-(n+k) permutations with exactly n external and k internal entries.

**Source.** Jacopo Borga. *Random Permutations – A geometric point of view*. 2021. Advisor(s): Mathilde Bouvel and Valentin Féray. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.09699) Location: Conjecture 5.1.4(e), p. 127.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1887, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution of this superlinear regime found.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Normalized permutation-rank empirical measures converge weakly in probability to Lebesgue whenever k/n -> infinity. Stronger range: every n>=4, without requiring n -> infinity. Scope: Uniform permutation of n+k entries with exactly n external records (an empty strict quadrant) and k internal entries; normalized permutation ranks, not arbitrary independent continuous coordinates. Restrictions/extensions: No narrowing: the proved theorem additionally allows fixed n>=4 while k/n tends to infinity. Remaining: No mathematical gap identified in the complete written argument. Thesis question-page numbering remains attachment provenance. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/B6_Research_Report_2026-10-08.md).

<a id="q1888"></a>

## Q1888. Complete hub mass

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Is ∑\_{i≥1}Z_i=1 almost surely?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1888, 1889, 1890: §7; normalization motivated by Krapivsky–Redner (2017). Setup for questions 1888, 1889, 1890: Start T₂ with edge {1,2}. Given T_n, choose a uniform vertex V and then its uniform neighbor W; attach n+1 to W. Let Z_i=lim_n deg_{T_n}(i)/n and N_n count nonleaves.

**Source.** Louigi Addario-Berry, Simon Briend, Luc Devroye, Serte Donderwinkel, Céline Kerriou and Gábor Lugosi. *Random Friend Trees*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20185) Location: §7(4).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1888, 1889, 1890, checked 6 October 2026: July journal version retains these questions.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: Sum_i Z_i=1 almost surely. Also l1 limits for leaf mass, excess-degree mass, and degree mass on nonleaves; TV attachment-label convergence, a conditional TV distance-law limit, and tight typical distances. Scope: T_2 is edge {1,2}; select a uniform vertex, then a uniform neighbor, and attach the new vertex to that neighbor. Z_i=lim degree(i)/n. Remaining: Depends on the primary polynomial nonleaf bound; no external peer review or formal verification claimed. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/B6_Research_Report_2026-10-08.md).

<a id="q1890"></a>

## Q1890. Nonleaf normalization

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Do μ>0 and a nondegenerate random variable X exist with n^(−μ)N_n→X almost surely?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1888, 1889, 1890: §7; normalization motivated by Krapivsky–Redner (2017). Setup for questions 1888, 1889, 1890: Start T₂ with edge {1,2}. Given T_n, choose a uniform vertex V and then its uniform neighbor W; attach n+1 to W. Let Z_i=lim_n deg_{T_n}(i)/n and N_n count nonleaves.

**Source.** Louigi Addario-Berry, Simon Briend, Luc Devroye, Serte Donderwinkel, Céline Kerriou and Gábor Lugosi. *Random Friend Trees*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.20185) Location: §7(1).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1888, 1889, 1890, checked 6 October 2026: July journal version retains these questions.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: With q_n=N_n^{-1} sum_{v:D_n(v)>=2} L_n(v)/D_n(v), log N_n-sum_{k=3}^{n-1} q_k/k has a finite real almost-sure limit. Scope: The same random-friend tree; N_n is the number of degree-at-least-two vertices; deterministic mu>0 and an almost-sure nondegenerate normalized limit are requested. Remaining: A deterministic exponent, convergence of the centered harmonic drift, and nondegeneracy remain. Positive finite power-normalized limits are equivalent to real convergence of the centered harmonic series. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b6-2026-10-08/B6_Research_Report_2026-10-08.md).

<a id="q1893"></a>

## Q1893. Critical memory-tree scaling

**Status:** Open, partial results · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

For β=1/2, does n^(−1/2)T_n converge in distribution in the Gromov–Hausdorff topology to a nondegenerate compact random metric space?

**Context.** Origin for question 1893: Authors’ open question in §3.3(d). Setup for question 1893: Start with vertex 1. For fixed β∈(0,1), vertex n+1 attaches uniformly to {max(1,n−⌊n^β⌋),…,n}. Equip the resulting T_n with graph distance.

**Source.** Omer Angel, Shankar Bhamidi, Serte Donderwinkel, Neeladri Maitra and Akshay Sakanaveeti. *Evolution of recursive trees with limited memory*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18856) Location: §3.3(d), p. 8.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1893, checked 6 October 2026: first-order results leave these limits open.

**Results.**

- **Partial result**: At beta=1/2, ||max_{v<=n}|D_v-4 sqrt(v)|/sqrt(n)||\_2 -> 0. Uniform-vertex radial measure converges to the law of 4 sqrt(U); any rooted measured subsequential limit has this law and height 4. Scope: Root 1; independently attach vertex n+1 uniformly to {max(1,n-floor(n^beta)),...,n}; beta=1/2 and graph distance, with bare GH convergence requested. Restrictions/extensions: No GH convergence asserted. A root and uniform-vertex measure are retained only for the necessary radial-profile statement. Remaining: GH tightness/compactness, the full metric object, and uniqueness remain; root-distance control is not a covering-number bound. Filed from the external B6 return; scope reading and selected controls do not constitute an independent full proof audit or a formal certificate. ## Q1894. Intermediate zero-range relaxation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.2) · **Collection** 19

For each fixed 1≤α<2, are there 0&lt;c_α≤C_α<∞ such that c_α n²(1+k/n)^α≤W_α(n,k)≤C_α n²(1+k/n)^α for every n≥3 and k≥1?

**Context.** Origin for question 1894: Conjecture 2.2; retain 1≤α<2. Setup for question 1894: On Z/nZ, k≥1 particles evolve in continuous time. A site containing r particles sends one to each neighbor at rate g_α(r)/2, where g_1(r)=log(r+1) and g_α(r)=((r+1)^(1−α)−1)/(1−α). Let W_α(n,k) be the inverse spectral gap on the k-particle sector.

**Source.** Freddy Hernández-Romero, Milton Jara and Fábio Júlio da Silva Valentim. *Relaxation time for a parametric family of zero-range processes*. 2025. [primary source](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40324-025-00389-5) Location: Conjecture 2.2, §2.4.

<a id="q1895"></a>

## Q1895. Sukhatme fixed points

**Status:** Solved here: proved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Determine centering, scaling and a nondegenerate limiting distribution for F_n as n→∞.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1895, 1896, 1897: Luce (1959) model; Sukhatme weights; §5.2–§5.3 questions. Setup for questions 1895, 1896, 1897: Give labels i∈{1,…,n} weights w_i=n−i+1. Sample without replacement, each next label with probability proportional to its remaining weight, and write the draw order as σ\_n. Let F_n, C_n and L_n denote its fixed-point count, total cycle count and longest increasing subsequence length.

**Source.** Jacopo Borga, Sourav Chatterjee and Persi Diaconis. *Permuton and local limits for the Luce model*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.07729v2) Location: §5.2, p. 35 (arXiv v2).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1895, 1896, 1897, checked 6 October 2026: the primary paper states these questions; no later resolution located.

**Results.**

- **Proved**: (F_n-(log n)/e)/sqrt((log n)/e) converges in distribution to N(0,1); E F_n=(log n)/e+O(1). Scope: Original Sukhatme fixed-point statistic. Assumptions: Weights n-i+1; reversing labels and ranks or taking inverse preserves the statistic. Remaining: Nothing remains at the requested centering/scaling/limit-law scope; no Poisson TV approximation, exact bounded mean correction, or variance asymptotic is asserted. Filed from the external B13 return; scope reading only, not an independent proof audit or a formal certificate. [Details](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/B13_Research_Report.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.07729v2)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 20

For every d≥2 and every such K, is p(K)≤p(B_d), where B_d is a Euclidean ball?

**Context.** Origin for question 1995: Glen Richard Hall, Acute triangles in the n-ball (1982). Setup for question 1995: For a bounded convex body K⊂R^d with nonempty interior, let p(K) be the probability that three independent uniformly sampled points form an acute triangle.

**Source.** Gabriel Khan. *Hall’s Conjecture on Extremal Sets for Random Triangles, Journal of Geometric Analysis 30*. 2020. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.05913) Location: §1.1, p. 2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1995, checked 6 October 2026: local extremality is proved; no global proof located.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s12220-019-00202-6) · [2](https://faculty.sites.iastate.edu/gkhan/project/halls-conjecture)


<a id="q1996"></a>

## Q1996. Functional Berry nodal-length limit

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question A) · **Collection** 20

Does X_E converge in distribution as E→∞ to the standard Brownian sheet in the multiparameter Skorokhod space D([0,1]²)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1996: Question A; earlier Peccati–Vidotto (2020), §4.3. Setup for question 1996: Let B_E be the centered planar Gaussian field with covariance J₀(2π√E|x−y|). Set L_E(t)=H¹({B_E=0}∩([0,t₁]×[0,t₂])) and X_E(t)=√(512π/log E)[L_E(t)−(π/√2)√E t₁t₂], t∈[0,1]².

**Source.** Massimo Notarnicola. *Giovanni Peccati and Anna Vidotto, Functional Convergence of Berry’s Nodal Lengths: Approximate Tightness and Total Disorder, 2023*. 2023. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.07580) Location: Question A, p. 5 of arXiv v2; (1.7).

**Literature check.** Status for question 1996, checked 6 October 2026: Gass–Peccati (December 2025) explicitly says Berry tightness remains open.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10955-023-03111-9) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17524)


<a id="q1997"></a>

## Q1997. Infinite second-moment worm threshold

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 1.11) · **Collection** 20

For d≥5, does E L²=∞ force v_c=0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1997, 1998, 1999, 2000: Question 1.11 and §1.5 of this work. Setup for questions 1997, 1998, 1999, 2000: At every vertex of Z^d independently start Poisson(v) simple random walks, each with iid positive-integer length L and trace {X₀,…,X_{L−1}}. Let v_c be the infimum v>0 for which the union of visited vertices has an infinite nearest-neighbor component with positive probability. Write m(ℓ)=P(L=ℓ).

**Source.** Balázs Ráth and Sándor Rokob. *Percolation of worms, 2021 preprint; SPA 152*. 2021. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2107.03259) Location: Question 1.11; Remark 1.10(vi), p. 6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1997, 1998, 1999, 2000, checked 6 October 2026: d≥5 question reaffirmed August 2026; no lower-dimensional resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.07145) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11198)

<a id="q2084"></a>

## Q2084. Uniqueness with two Markov marginals

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 9.1) · **Collection** 21

For every Hölder potential φ on the product shift, is the maximizer of h(λ)+∫φ dλ over these joinings unique?

**Context.** Origin for question 2084: Problem 9.1, explicit Markov special case in Remark 9.1. Setup for question 2084: On the two-sided full binary shift, let μ\_α,ν\_α be stationary Markov laws with transition matrices [[α,1−α],[1−α,α]] and [[1−α,α],[α,1−α]], respectively, 0<α<1. A joining is a shift-invariant probability law with these marginals; h is its entropy rate.

**Source.** Shengwen Guo. *Stationary Optimal Transport Plans and the Thermodynamic Formalism*. 2025. Advisor(s): Kevin McGoff. [primary source](https://ninercommons.charlotte.edu/record/26258/files/Guo_uncc_0694D_14270.pdf) Location: Remark 9.1, p. 105; Problem 9.1, p. 104.

**Literature check.** Status for question 2084, checked 6 October 2026: no resolution of this special case found.

**Further links.** [1](https://ninercommons.charlotte.edu/record/26258) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02435)


<a id="q2085"></a>

## Q2085. Uniform learnability to fluctuation tails

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

Can one explicitly construct, solely from ψ, a bound Φ with P(Jε≥Φ(λ,ε))≤λ for every process admitting ψ?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2085, 2086: Remark 5.2 and the first question preceding Problem 5.3. Setup for questions 2085, 2086: For real random variables Y_n, put Cε(a,b)={max_{a≤i,j≤b}|Y_i−Y_j|>ε}. A uniform learnable bound ψ satisfies: for every a₀&lt;b₀≤a₁&lt;b₁≤⋯ and λ,ε∈(0,1], some k≤ψ(λ,ε) has P(Cε(a_k,b_k))≤λ. Let Jε be the supremum of k with i₁&lt;j₁≤⋯≤i_k&lt;j_k and |Y_{i_l}−Y_{j_l}|>ε for every l.

**Source.** Morenikeji Neri. *Oscillations in the Strong Law of Large Numbers, 2026 preprint; Proceedings AMS forthcoming*. 2026. [primary source](https://kejineri.github.io/Papers/Oscillations_in_the_SLLN_submitted.pdf) Location: Remark 5.2, p. 17.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2085, 2086, checked 6 October 2026: current author manuscript retains these questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01676) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22741) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08078) · [4](https://nicholaspischke.github.io/bib/ref_name.html)


<a id="q2086"></a>

## Q2086. Remove lacunarity from pairwise-independent means

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 5.3) · **Collection** 21

For pairwise independent identically distributed X_n with E X_n=0 and E|X_n|≤μ<∞, do Y_n=(X₁+⋯+X_n)/n have an explicit uniform learnable bound depending only on μ,λ,ε, without restrictions on b_k/a_k?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2085, 2086: Remark 5.2 and the first question preceding Problem 5.3. Setup for questions 2085, 2086: For real random variables Y_n, put Cε(a,b)={max_{a≤i,j≤b}|Y_i−Y_j|>ε}. A uniform learnable bound ψ satisfies: for every a₀&lt;b₀≤a₁&lt;b₁≤⋯ and λ,ε∈(0,1], some k≤ψ(λ,ε) has P(Cε(a_k,b_k))≤λ. Let Jε be the supremum of k with i₁&lt;j₁≤⋯≤i_k&lt;j_k and |Y_{i_l}−Y_{j_l}|>ε for every l.

**Source.** Morenikeji Neri. *Oscillations in the Strong Law of Large Numbers, 2026 preprint; Proceedings AMS forthcoming*. 2026. [primary source](https://kejineri.github.io/Papers/Oscillations_in_the_SLLN_submitted.pdf) Location: §5, p. 19, first question preceding Problem 5.3; Theorem 1.5.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2085, 2086, checked 6 October 2026: current author manuscript retains these questions.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.01676) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.22741) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08078) · [4](https://nicholaspischke.github.io/bib/ref_name.html)


<a id="q2087"></a>

## Q2087. Small reinforcement in dimensions three to five

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 21

For every d∈{3,4,5}, is there a_d>0 such that this walk on Z^d is almost surely transient whenever 0&lt;a&lt;a_d?

**Context.** Origin for questions 2087, 2088, 2089: Lattice phase conjecture: Vladas Sidoravicius, circa 2010; first printed in Beffara’s 2011 habilitation. Setup for questions 2087, 2088, 2089: A walk starts at 0. Each undirected edge initially has weight 1, permanently changed to 1+a after its first crossing, a>0. At every discrete step it selects an incident edge proportionally to its current weight. Recurrence means infinitely many returns to 0.

**Source.** Dor Elboim and Gady Kozma. *Once-reinforced random walk in high dimensions, 2026*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.17972) Location: Introduction and §1.1, pp. 1–2.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 2087, 2088, 2089, checked 6 October 2026: September range paper still records the lattice gap; no strip resolution found.
